#5112. Michael Saylor prioritizes STRC buybacks as Strategy’s Bitcoin buying slows 90% – Why?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Nothing highlights the impact of ongoing market volatility better than Strategy.

In a recent interview, Michael Saylor outlined a potential shift in Strategy’s approach. He explained why the company could sell MSTR shares even below its net Bitcoin value to buy back STRC. The key takeaway? The market is calling this arguably the most revealing part of the 19th of November’s investor Q&A.

Fundamentally, this seems logical. As outlined below, MSTR has performed extremely poorly as Bitcoin has fallen, losing over 40% of value within Q4. If MSTR loses another 25% of value, it will have lost more this quarter than last year’s Q4 crash, potentially making it the worst quarter MSTR has had since the bear market back in 2022.

The result? Strategy’s Bitcoin [BTC] accumulation has slowed sharply in Q4 2026.

In Q4 2025, Strategy bought 32,470 BTC. In comparison, it has only bought 3,183 BTC so far in Q4 2026. This represents a drop of 90%. To date, Strategy also used $206.3 million proceeds from selling MSTR shares to buy STRC shares. However, only $33.4 million has been used to buy Bitcoin.

In short, broader market weakness is putting Strategy under pressure. Against this backdrop, Michael Saylor’s latest STRC strategy doesn’t look like a random move. Strategy seems to be shifting more capital toward supporting its preferred stock while navigating a difficult market. But with FUD around the company continuing to build, the bigger question is whether STRC can still attract enough investor demand to support Strategy’s Bitcoin accumulation plans.

One thing is clear: Strategy’s main priority is to keep STRC stable.

As one analyst pointed out, this could mean the company would continue selling MSTR shares to fund STRC buybacks. If that isn’t enough, Strategy could also sell Bitcoin whenever STRC trades below its $100 par value. Saylor believes STRC’s 12% dividend is already attractive enough, making it a key income-generating product for institutional investors.

But what does this mean for Strategy shareholders? As the chart below shows, major institutional investors increased their MSTR holdings in Q3 2026. The top 15 now own around 33% of all outstanding shares. This shows that big investors are still interested in MSTR despite market weakness.

However, with Strategy focusing more on STRC, will MSTR shareholders still benefit from its BTC strategy?

Notably, this is where a potential feedback loop comes in. Continued weakness in MSTR could push Strategy to rely more on STRC buybacks to support its capital structure. However, this could also put institutional demand for MSTR at risk. If investor confidence weakens, raising fresh capital for future STRC buybacks could become more difficult.

So, for now, Saylor’s shift towards STRC signals a “risky” change in how Strategy plans to fund future BTC purchases.

Strategy is focusing more on STRC buybacks as MSTR struggles, potentially slowing Bitcoin purchases.

STRC’s 12% dividend may attract investors, but can it maintain demand without hurting MSTR?

#5111. $1 SHIB Sent to Dead Wallets in Shiba Inu Burn Lag

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Shibburn’s page shows that just $1 worth of SHIB has been burned over the past 24 hours, indicating a decrease in Shiba Inu’s token-burning activity.

The daily burn rate has dropped by 37.21% due to a burn of 303,294 SHIB worth just $1 over the last day.

The latest figure suggests that very little SHIB was destroyed during the reported period, although an increase in burn rate was seen on a weekly time frame.

157.31 million SHIB were burned in the last seven days, with the burn rate increasing by 113%; a different scenario presents itself on a 30-day basis, when the monthly burn rate fell 48.87% with 343.11 million SHIB burned.

Burns involve sending tokens to inaccessible wallet addresses, effectively taking them out of the available supply, with Shiba Inu advancing this course as 41.08% of its initial supply has now been erased.

A total of 410,845,365,512,059 SHIB has been burned in 21,978 transactions, according to Shibburn.

Shiba Inu fell to a fresh low of $0.00000337 as widespread selling picked up across the crypto market.

At the time of writing, SHIB remains down 5.73% over the last 24 hours to $0.00000362. Just below SHIB's current price, a large buy wall has emerged, with Binance's spot order book revealing 17.92 trillion SHIB positioned at $0.00000361.

SHIB is exhibiting a strong accumulation of buy orders, which could signal a key support level as traders monitor buyers’ ability to soak up selling pressure.

A buy wall occurs when a lot of limit buy orders are stacked up around some price level and can show strong buying interest, providing temporary support if market participants are willing to fill those orders.

Now, if the price of Shiba Inu goes lower, then the 17.92 trillion SHIB order concentration that was reported could be an important area to watch. If the orders get filled and remain there, they could help absorb some of the selling pressure.

#5110. VanEck ends Gemini deal for 3 crypto ETFs – What’s next for VSOL?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

VanEck has terminated Gemini as custodian and clearing agent for three U.S. crypto ETFs. In doing so, it has made Coinbase Custody its sole remaining safekeeper, thus putting into jeopardy the Solana fund’s staking future.

As per three 8-K filings from the 19th of November, VanEck Digital Assets delivered written termination notices to Gemini Trust Company on the 16th of November, with the contract set to officially end on 16th February.

The notice ended custody and clearing arrangements for the VanEck Bitcoin ETF (HODL) and the VanEck Ethereum ETF (ETHV).

The third ETF, the VanEck Solana ETF (VSOL), is the most affected one in this regard, because there is no news on who will take over staking services for it. Market participants are assuming it is Coinbase, but as of now, there is no confirmation yet.

As expected, the filings have opened a series of questions, as they have not established the allocation of assets between Gemini and Coinbase. This, in turn, has made it impossible to quantify precisely what Gemini is losing in dollar terms.

The filing also failed to explain why VanEck chose to terminate the agreements now, with roughly three months of notice before the effective date. Moreover, they did not even address the staking question that matters most for VSOL shareholders.

This comes as the crypto ETF landscape has been in a consolidation for a while now, with Coinbase already being a custodian for most U.S. Spot crypto ETFs.

All in all, VanEck’s current decision to put an end to its Gemini arrangements has left it with a single custodian across all three funds and a massive unresolved operational gap.

What’s left now is that by 16th February, 2027, the firm will have to find a new staking provider for VSOL.

#5109. Cardano Lifeline? 3.96 Million ADA Buy Wall Emerges on Binance

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Cardano fell to $0.133, a five-month low on Saturday as the crypto market sell-off deepened. At the time of writing, Cardano had slightly rebounded, up 1.82% in the last 24 hours to $0.144, as a substantial buy wall of 3.96 million ADA emerged on Binance, offering a layer of support as sellers pressured the cryptocurrency.

A buy wall occurs when a large volume of buy orders is concentrated around a particular price level on an exchange's order book. Such an order cluster can indicate that traders are prepared to purchase an asset at that price, potentially slowing further declines if selling pressure meets sufficient demand.

In Cardano's case, the 3.96 million ADA order cluster that occurred below the $0.14 level revealed a notable level of buying interest. As the orders at this level were filled, they helped absorb selling pressure for Cardano and provided short-term support for the price.

Current data from CoinGlass shows that open interest is down 1.98%, mostly due to shorts closing, although funding remains negative at −0.0099%, with shorts paying longs.

The next key levels to watch for ADA, according to on-chain data provided by CoinGlass, are $0.1462, which has a short liquidation cluster, and $0.1375, which has a long liquidation cluster.

Dijkstra hard fork preparations continue to progress, with Intersect, a Cardano-based entity, revealing what might come next in the coming weeks.

in about one or two weeks, the Cardano Node 11.2.3 release (GitHub), the Node RC 2026w47 release for the Musashi network (GitHub), and the publication of v6 of CAP-12 to the Constitutional Amendment Portal are anticipated.

In about two to three weeks, the Cardano Node 11.3 pre-release is anticipated, while an update to the Constitution governance action on-chain is expected in about one month.

Dijkstra requires both constitutional and technical changes in the era of on-chain governance. These include Constitutional guardrails (Update Constitution) and Plutus V4 (Parameter Update).

CAP-12 "Dijkstra era parameters" proposes to amend the Constitution to accommodate new block type definitions and set guardrails for new updatable parameter types introduced with Protocol Version 12.

#5108. XRP & XLM Hit Closest To ‘Value Area’ Among Major-Caps

Home » Altcoins » XRP & XLM Hit Closest To ‘Value Area’ Among Major-Caps

These two major-cap altcoin peers are hitting close to historically-undervalued territory: a highly-awaited bottom sign?

French analyst EGRAG compared the 4-hour charts of XRP, Ethereum, Hedera, Bitcoin, Solana, and Stellar to assess which assets are showing relative strength after recent lows.

The mainstream French crypto chart analyst’s review centers on price reaction, volume-profile location, and the distance remaining to each coin’s Value Area Low (VAL).

XRP is holding above its recent low without returning for a full retest. Solana is behaving in a similar fashion, maintaining its recovery structure. Stellar (XLM) is tracking XRP but appears slightly weaker. Ethereum (ETH) is retesting its lows, with a potential higher low developing.

Bitcoin (BTC) also retested and printed a slightly higher high. Hedera’s HBAR was retested with a lower high, the most shaky local structure among the six.

Distance to reclaim the VAL currently ranks as follows:

XRP and Stellar (XLM) are the nearest to re-entering their value areas. Bitcoin shows the more constructive local recovery structure among the larger-cap assets, even though it remains farther from its VAL than the two payment-focused names.

A double bottom is not confirmed simply because price holds above a prior low. The analysis treats bottoming as a process rather than a single candle. Early strength is marked by reclaiming the VAL.

#CRYPTO – 6 COINS, WHO IS SHOWING STRENGTH? #XRP – #ETH – #HBAR – #BTC – #SOL – #XLM FOLLOW THE PRICE, VOLUME & STRUCTURE! 👀The 4H charts reveal different reactions after the recent lows:▫️ #XRP: Holding above its recent low without a full retest.▫️ #ETH: Retesting the… pic.twitter.com/IFPBMuh7NJ

Structural confirmation requires a break of the local high. Stronger bullish conviction needs a reclaim of the Point of Control (POC) accompanied by volume.

The 4-hour volume profiles show each asset still trading below or near the lower boundary of its recent value area. Acceptance back inside the value area, followed by a move toward the POC, would shift the local bias.

Until that occurs, the charts remain in a recovery attempt rather than a confirmed reversal.

As of November 19, 2026, approximate prices stood near 63,700–64,000 for Bitcoin, $1,810 for Ethereum, $0.99 for XRP, $75 for Solana (SOL), $0.138 for Stellar (XLM) & $0.056 for Hedera Hashgraph (HBAR).

The broader market remains under moderate pressure, with Bitcoin dominance elevated and the Fear & Greed Index in Extreme Fear territory for consecutive days now.

The XRP coin & Stellar Lumens (XLM) are the nearest to early strength signals. Bitcoin’s slightly higher high on the retest is the more constructive structural note among the majors.

Confirmation across the group still requires a break of local highs and a return into the value area with volume. Until those conditions are met, the recovery remains unconfirmed.

#5107. Palmer Luckey Says He’s Building an Offshore Haven for Industry—And 'Illegal' Medicine

Palmer Luckey Says He’s Building an Offshore Haven for Industry—And 'Illegal' Medicine

Palmer Luckey, the billionaire founder of Oculus and defense technology company Anduril, is spending over a billion dollars to have a private island where he can do things without worrying about U.S. laws.

He wants to build a massive industrial platform in the ocean where American businesses can operate without some of the regulations they face on land. And he has apparently been working on the idea for nearly a decade.

In an X post published Wednesday, Luckey revealed a project called SLAB, a proposed seastead with a 1,000-year design life that he says could support industries ranging from metal processing and oil refining to medical research and treatment.

Selling Oculus to Facebook in 2014 gave me money for helicopter flight lessons and developing a seastead called SLAB with a 1,000 year design life.

The goal was to be cheaper than land and absent state/local laws that cripple critical American industries like metal processing,… pic.twitter.com/aJkd0eOgLF

— Palmer Luckey (@PalmerLuckey) November 18, 2026

His goal, he wrote, is to create an industrial base cheaper than land and free from state and local laws that he believes are crippling critical American industries. Luckey described the project as a way to strengthen the United States rather than escape it, calling it a "relief valve" for outdated regulations.

“Me and a few close friends cashed it up at a $1B valuation back in 2017, and while it still has a long way to go, there has been significant progress towards my goal of reviving manifest destiny and westward expansion,” he wrote.

Luckey said he used proceeds from Facebook's 2014 acquisition of Oculus to finance the project.

The announcement included an AI-generated illustration of a gigantic offshore platform, rather than photographs of an operating facility so it’s hard to know which type of endeavor he’s dedicating his time and money to.

Luckey said he decided to disclose SLAB after learning that journalists were preparing to describe it as a floating AI data center. He rejected that characterization as incomplete, saying the project was considerably older and "way cooler than that."

Luckey provided a more specific indication of his ambitions in a heated exchange with journalist Joshua Topolsky, who questioned whether creating an offshore community was a sensible way to modernize medical research.

Topolsky argued that regulatory reform could happen without constructing an island in the ocean and challenged Luckey to identify which existing rules had prevented him from pursuing medical advances.

Luckey responded that he would rather build a place where people could save their families with "safe but currently illegal medicine ASAP" than wait for lawmakers to reform the system.

He accused political interests and large pharmaceutical companies of obstructing change, arguing that conventional reform would require legislation capable of securing support from 60 senators.

Luckey's plan also faces a significant legal complication: moving an operation into the ocean does not automatically place it outside American jurisdiction.

When another X user asked how close SLAB could operate to San Francisco without falling under its jurisdiction, Luckey answered, "Three miles."

That generally corresponds to the boundary of California's state-controlled coastal waters, according to the U.S. Bureau of Ocean Energy Management.

That said, federal jurisdiction extends considerably farther. The United States has a 12-nautical-mile territorial sea and a 200-nautical-mile exclusive economic zone, where it retains authority over certain economic activities and the construction and use of artificial islands, according to NOAA guidance.

The applicable rules would depend on SLAB's location, whether it floats or is attached to the seabed, and how it is registered. Even vessels operating on the high seas remain subject to applicable maritime laws and the jurisdiction of their flag states, so it’s not as if he’d be living in an anarchist fairy tale.

Medical research presents its own complications, if that’s what he’s actually doing. The FDA requires foreign clinical studies submitted in support of U.S. drug approvals to meet good clinical practice standards, including independent ethical review and informed consent. Conducting treatment offshore would not, by itself, make an unapproved drug legal for sale in the United States.

Previous seasteading experiments have encountered similar jurisdictional disputes. A floating community off Thailand triggered a confrontation with authorities in 2019, while a subsequent project in Panama adopted boat registration to operate within an existing legal framework.

This is all, of course, speculation. Luckey has not publicly identified SLAB's proposed location, the specific regulations it would avoid, or the medical treatments he hopes to make available. He has also not announced a construction deadline or provided independently verifiable details of the project's current physical development.

#5106. Ethereum Foundation Sells ETH as Bitcoin (BTC) Price Drops to 2-Year Low

The Ethereum Foundation-linked wallet dumped another 2,500 $ETH ($3.88M), according to the Arkham database.

Wallet “0x5CBC” holds around $44.8 million in crypto, including $38.4 million of SUSDS and $6.41 million of Ethereum. Its latest transfer was on November 21, 2026.

Previously, another two wallets linked to the Ethereum Foundation transferred 4,393 ETH ($11.1 million at that time) to the Kraken exchange on October 12.

This transactions are not impulsive moves but part of a carefully planned 10-year strategy. Over the past decade, a total of 49,751 ETH has been sent from these addresses to exchanges.

The average selling price was a modest $658 by today's standards, although it’s worth noting that these coins were originally acquired in the project's early days, when ETH was worth less than a dollar.

Ethereum is currently going through one of its most challenging times in recent memory. Investor sentiment is still low, network activity has dispersed throughout Layer-2 solutions.

In addition to that, Ethereum has spent most of 2026 underperforming many rival ecosystems.

Earlier this year, Hsiao-Wei Wang has stepped down from the roles of co-executive director and board member of the Ethereum Foundation. In February, fellow co-Executive Director Tomasz Stańczak also stepped down.

Ethereum foundation has been selling ETH in 2026 during steep price movements, both up and down. For instance, BitMine acquired 10,000 ETH at an average price of $2,292 this June.

A similar sale one week earlier, where another 10,000 ETH was sold at $2,387 per coin.

On Friday, Bitcoin price dropped below $60,000. At some point, it fell 5.10% to $55,830, the lowest level since September 2024.

Liquidations on crypto exchanges reached $1.57 billion over 24 hours on Nov. 20. According to CoinGlass, 265,525 traders were liquidated over 24 hours, and long positions accounted for $1.43 billion of the total.

Bitcoin saw $554.28 million in liquidations, and 91.3% of that amount came from long positions.

Ethereum fell below $1,700 on Nov. 20, and ETH traders saw $331.08 million in liquidations.

#5105. Bitcoin’s price hits ‘bottom territory’ after BTC falls below $60K — What next?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Less than half of all Bitcoin [BTC] is green right now, and that’s not a good look. In fact, as it turns out, we may not see light anytime soon.

As of 18th November, those are the numbers. At least according to the Rand Group.

Every major BTC bottom since 2015 has happened with less than half the supply in the green. In August 2025, that figure was 35.7%. It hit 39.8% in December 2018, 42.5% in March 2020, and 44.8% in November 2022.

Each low has come in a little higher than the one before. Holders are giving up less ground with every cycle. The argument is that the best opportunities show up when most holders are underwater, not when everyone’s celebrating.

Hence, the question: Does that mean the bottom is in? No, But Bitcoin is in a zone where that has mattered before.

… might be tech stocks and rates, according to QCP Capital.

Not everyone’s ready to call it a bottom, yet. In fact, the digital asset partner thinks Bitcoin’s problem is about money getting tight everywhere.

Earlier this cycle, ETF buying, corporate treasuries, and hopes of rate cuts did a good job of soaking up selling. That support will crack when stocks drop across the board.

So, what would it take to turn things around?

…recovery will likely require stabilisation in technology equities, reduced volatility in rates and evidence that institutional flows are returning.

Until then, any bounce driven by shorts closing out could fizzle fast. The Fed could also be another weight on BTC’s back.

At the time of writing, the charts weren’t really comforting either. BTC was trading at $58,681, down 5.2% over the last 24 hours. Just a couple of weeks ago, it was trading above $80K.

The RSI was knee deep in oversold territory, its lowest reading in months. Sellers have been running out fuel, but oversold conditions can stay as such for a while.

The ADX was 43.8 and still rising. So, the downtrend seemed to be getting stronger. Especially since buyers didn’t look like they were going to turn up.

This is a bottom range that we know. However, until the selling pressure slows down, calling it the floor would be a gamble.

#5104. Binance Hack-Linked Wallet Moves 34,279 ETH, but Investigators See No Confirmed Sale

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

A wallet linked by blockchain investigators to the October 29 Binance breach has transferred 34,279.66 ETH to three newly created addresses, ending a period of inactivity. The ether was worth roughly $63 million at prices used in Tuesday’s reporting. That is a significant movement of suspected stolen funds, but a wallet transfer is not automatically a sale, a successful laundering step or proof that the recipient controls are independent of the sender. Investigators had not identified the new addresses as exchanges at the time of their initial reports.

Monitoring groups MistTrack and Solid Intel reported the transfers on November 17. MistTrack described three movements of approximately 12,000 ETH, 12,000 ETH and 10,279.66 ETH. The receiving wallets were newly created, and the monitored funds had not moved again when the analysts published their findings. Public transaction records make it possible to trace the amounts and destinations, but they do not disclose the human operator behind each address. The link to the earlier breach rests on investigators’ attribution of the sending wallet, not a label embedded in the blockchain itself.

The October attack involved vastly larger losses than Tuesday’s movement. US investigators attributed the incident to a North Korean hacking group, and authorities have announced substantial asset restraints and seizures. Blockchain intelligence firms have also tracked funds moved through different assets and protocols. These parallel developments should not be added together casually, because frozen, seized, recovered and still-controlled amounts describe different legal and operational states. The fresh ETH transfers are one piece of a complex post-incident trail rather than an update to the full net-loss number.

For related context, see our earlier report on Coinbase's custody and security precautions.

Crypto trading commentary sometimes treats any transfer from an exploit-linked wallet as preparation to dump tokens. That can happen, but the evidence here does not yet establish a market sale. Transfers may consolidate funds, redistribute control or precede another operation. Until an address is linked to a trading venue, bridge, mixer or other service, the immediate observable fact is the destination change. Even a later exchange deposit would need careful interpretation: deposits are not identical to executed sell orders. Reporting should preserve those steps rather than collapsing them into one dramatic conclusion.

Ethereum’s public ledger lets investigators monitor known addresses and alert service providers if assets reappear at identifiable points. Token issuers, exchanges and law enforcement may each have different powers depending on the assets and jurisdictions involved. Native ETH cannot simply be frozen by an issuer in the way some centrally administered tokens can. That difference complicates recovery when funds leave services that can intervene. The next useful evidence will be further movements, identification of services receiving the funds and official updates to the case.

Investigators can improve attribution confidence by comparing the sequence, amounts and transaction timing with addresses already associated with the incident. Yet public labels can be incomplete, and a newly created wallet may simply be another address controlled by the same actor. Readers should resist claims that funds have reached a criminal group, a particular country or a cash-out desk unless that identification is supported by additional evidence. Future investigative notices should specify their degree of confidence and distinguish observed transfers from hypotheses about their purpose.

For related context, see our earlier report on the Unocoin wallet compromise.

The latest transfer warrants close monitoring, but it should not be reported as the disposal of $63 million in stolen ether without further proof. The distinction between observable transactions and inferred intent is especially important in a security incident of this scale.

Reporting sources: CoinDesk report of MistTrack and Solid Intel monitoring · MistTrack transfer report

#5103. Why Bitcoin Just Dropped to a 5-Month Low Near $61,300

Home » Bitcoin (BTC) » Why Bitcoin Just Dropped to a 5-Month Low Near $61,300

Three separate market pressures just collided—pushing BTC to a level not seen since July.

Bitcoin plunged 4.35% over the past 24 hours, falling to $61,300 — its lowest level since early July — as a global sell-off in AI stocks, heavy withdrawals from Bitcoin ETFs and a wave of forced liquidations sent shockwaves through crypto markets.

By Friday morning, Bitcoin was struggling to stay above $61,500, putting it on track for its seventh consecutive weekly decline.

This drop wasn’t triggered by a single event. Instead, several pressures hit the market at once: growing concerns over AI valuations, institutional investors pulling money out of Bitcoin ETFs, and leveraged traders being forced to close their positions as prices fell.

The broader pullback was driven by a retreat from riskier assets as investors questioned lofty AI valuations.

On Thursday, November 19, AI company Anthropic made its highly anticipated debut on the Nasdaq. Shares opened at $111.50 against an IPO price of $83, briefly pushing the company’s valuation above $2 trillion. However, the successful IPO failed to lift sentiment across the technology sector.

As Anthropic began trading, Bitcoin fell from around $62,700 to $62,250 within roughly 30 minutes, while broader Nasdaq losses accelerated.

By the end of the session, the Nasdaq was down 2.3%, the S&P 500 had fallen 2%, and the Dow Jones lost 2.2%.

The sell-off spread to Asia, where South Korea’s KOSPI dropped approximately 5%, triggering a temporary restriction on automated selling.

Institutional demand for Bitcoin is also showing signs of weakening, adding further pressure to an already struggling market.

According to SoSoValue data, U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded $582.19 million in net outflows on November 19, marking the largest single-day withdrawal of the month.

With the exception of a modest $8 million inflow on November 5, U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs have faced nearly two weeks of persistent capital withdrawals, signaling a significant pullback in institutional demand.

As spot prices broke below key support levels, leveraged derivatives traders fueled a cascade of forced selling.

CoinGlass data shows $697.35 million in crypto positions were liquidated over the past 24 hours. About $600M came from long positions, and $201.25M of the total was Bitcoin longs.

According to market analyst Axel Adler Jr., Bitcoin remains under significant bearish pressure, with short-term investors sitting on substantial unrealized losses. Bitcoin is currently trading 15.7% below the average cost basis of recent buyers and has remained below that level for 12 consecutive days.

Until BTC reclaims the short-term holder cost basis and U.S. spot ETF flows flip back into positive territory, an analyst warns that the broader market correction isn’t over yet.

Bitcoin’s slide alongside AI stocks underscores how tightly crypto still tracks risk appetite in traditional markets. Continued ETF outflows and leveraged liquidations are likely to keep pressure on prices until broader market sentiment stabilizes.

The daily chart of XRP has yielded a death cross, the first time the signal has popped up in 2026. XRP experienced a death cross on its daily chart in November 2025 and then entered a downtrend for several months before a recovery in August 2026.

Since then, the wider crypto market has struggled. The declines deepened in late October, as concerns over artificial intelligence valuations and spending fueled a broad tech sell-off. They were compounded by the $6.3 billion Binance hack and weakening institutional ETF demand.

#5101. US Senators Question SEC Tokenized-Stock Exemption Over Wallet Transfers and Investor Protection

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Four Democratic US senators have pressed the Securities and Exchange Commission for answers about how its new approach to tokenized stock trading will protect investors and prevent illicit transfers. Their November 16 letter, reported by The Block, takes aim at gaps they see in the SEC’s five-year innovation exemption for certain trading venues. The development is an oversight challenge, not a court ruling or a notice that the exemption has been cancelled. It goes to a central difficulty for tokenized securities: a regulated purchase can become a blockchain transfer to another address.

The SEC introduced its approach in September, allowing qualifying tokenized-securities venues to operate under a defined exemption from some ordinary exchange-registration requirements. The aim is to permit experimentation with trading stocks in token form while the agency assesses a new market structure. A token representing a security still raises questions about ownership, trading rules and investor rights. The exemptions and their precise conditions matter more than a general statement that securities have moved on-chain. Not every blockchain token, broker or venue automatically qualifies for the same regulatory treatment.

Senators Angela Alsobrooks, Ruben Gallego, Mark Warner and Catherine Cortez Masto reportedly told SEC Chair Paul Atkins that the framework lacks sufficiently specific safeguards around know-your-customer and anti-money-laundering procedures. A buyer verified by a regulated venue might later transfer a token to a wallet whose controller has not undergone the same checks. This problem is not solved merely by verifying the identity of the first purchaser. The letter asks how secondary transfers and the reach of compliance obligations would be managed once tokens circulate beyond the initial venue.

For related context, see our earlier report on Hong Kong's developing licensing rules.

The senators raised concerns about retail-investor protections and pricing, and reportedly asked whether companies could opt out of having their shares tokenized. Those questions are distinct from financial-crime screening. A token that references equity needs a clear relationship to the rights and obligations of the underlying security, as well as fair information about execution and ownership. If an issuer’s wishes, the investor’s expectations and the platform’s legal structure do not line up, technical transferability alone cannot resolve the conflict. Policymakers must address those issues in enforceable terms.

The lawmakers asked the SEC to explain its approach and urged a transparent rulemaking process for changes in the regulatory treatment of tokenized securities. Such a letter can affect oversight and public debate, especially as control of congressional committees changes, but it is not itself binding legislation. Venues exploring tokenized equities should continue to follow the actual exemption terms and any subsequent SEC guidance. The next concrete milestones are the agency’s response, proposed revisions if any, and how early trading models implement identity, custody and transfer restrictions.

For related context, see our earlier report on new institutional crypto-custody arrangements.

Tokenization can change how securities move, but it does not automatically settle which rules should accompany them. The dispute now concerns the design of that regulatory perimeter, with no basis to describe the senators’ letter as an immediate prohibition on tokenized stocks.

Reporting sources: The Block report of four senators’ letter

#5100. Why TRUMP memecoin faces THESE risks ahead of Donald Trump’s exclusive dinner

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

What exactly could keep investors from maintaining their conviction in TRUMP?

For context, U.S. President Donald Trump is set to host an exclusive dinner for top holders of his TRUMP memecoin on Sunday. The timing is interesting. Politically, the Democrats’ victory in the midterm elections adds another layer of uncertainty for TRUMP holders. It could also increase scrutiny of Trump’s crypto-related ventures, making political developments an important factor to watch.

Another issue to note is that 10 wallets make up 80.6% of the overall token supply. So, while there are over 691k holders of TRUMP, a large percentage of these tokens are concentrated within a few wallets.

Against this backdrop, it’s no surprise that President Trump is hosting this dinner.

The logic is simple: If any large holders (there are 10 holders holding >80% of TRUMP) begin to sell TRUMP, it may cause panic selling. This risk is increased as TRUMP is already facing macroeconomic uncertainty.

So, is the dinner a “strategic” attempt to bring back interest in the memecoin?

The speculative capital in this cycle isn’t flowing into the memecoin market.

This marks a key difference from previous cycles, when investors often turned to memecoins during volatile market conditions to chase quick gains. But this time, the trend looks different. Despite broader market weakness, the memecoin market cap has continued to decline, with over $20 billion wiped out in Q4 so far.

But could President Trump’s dinner initiative change that? From a technical standpoint, TRUMP is deeply oversold, trading 99% below its all-time high and recently breaking below the key $1 level. If the dinner sparks fresh momentum, traders could look to take advantage of these lower price levels ahead of the event.

According to AMBCrypto, this could bring some speculative capital back into memecoins.

However, sustainability is a concern here. Traders often come in and out of memecoins, so there may be little sustainability in this position in the long-term. Furthermore, as 80% of Official Trump [TRUMP] supply is concentrated in only 10 wallets, traders may be cautious of building up spot exposure.

Alternatively, they could favor short-term speculative trades to capitalize on  any event-driven price action. This could lead to increased speculation. However, due to weak market conditions, it may cause a new wave of de-leveraging and put more pressure on the larger crypto market.

TRUMP’s supply concentration and political uncertainty could weaken investor confidence, increasing the risk of sudden sell-offs.

Trump’s dinner may revive short-term memecoin speculation, but weak market conditions could trigger fresh deleveraging.

#5098. ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini and Grok Helped Plan Terror Attacks in Government Tests

ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini and Grok Helped Plan Terror Attacks in Government Tests

Four of the most widely used AI chatbots helped government investigators develop potentially deadly attack plans in less than 12 hours, despite safeguards intended to stop exactly that kind of request.

OpenAI's ChatGPT, Anthropic's Claude, Google's Gemini and Grok all supplied usable information during covert testing by the Government Accountability Office, according to a report released Wednesday. Investigators posed as attackers, then checked the responses against real locations, available materials, and expert assessments.

Two systems assisted with plans involving improvised explosives in crowded U.S. cities. Two others supplied information for attacks on electrical infrastructure, according to the watchdog, which withheld the models' assignments and operational details to prevent copycat attempts.

Senator Maggie Hassan, the New Hampshire Democrat who requested the investigation, warned that a terrorist could obtain "a concrete playbook for how to carry out a deadly attack" in less than 12 hours, according to her statement.

The chatbots initially activated protections, including refusing requests, removing harmful information or suspending accounts. Investigators bypassed those barriers with common jailbreak techniques, which manipulate an AI system into ignoring its safety rules.

The GAO checked suggested locations, researched whether materials were available and consulted specialists about the plans' feasibility. Some answers contained errors, including misplaced roads on a map, and investigators did not construct explosives or execute an attack.

In the report, "viable" means somebody would likely be able to follow the information supplied, not that an attack was demonstrated to succeed. Each model was tested on one assigned scenario, and the agency said the results were not statistically representative or suitable for ranking the companies.

OpenAI and Google said the tests involved older models, while Anthropic acknowledged that determined users can sometimes extract harmful material. The companies described continuing improvements to safety systems, though the GAO concluded those protections failed in its tests.

This is not really new. The findings follow a previous research showing eight of 10 popular chatbots assisted simulated teenage users planning violent attacks. The separate study tested 720 responses and found the bots provided actionable help roughly three-quarters of the time.

The GAO findings arrive amid outages affecting banks, Microsoft services and the National Security Agency. Officials say the disruptions were separate incidents, not evidence of a coordinated AI-driven cyberattack, even as the rush to repair software flaws has created its own problems.

Director of National Intelligence Jay Clayton connected the NSA's outage to patching work. Officials described the recent breakdowns as maintenance-related, although individual organizations offered different explanations for their failures.

AI tools now find vulnerabilities faster than human teams can test and deploy fixes. Last month, the AI-assisted discovery of critical PHP flaws pushed website operators into an urgent patching campaign; the software powers a large share of the web.

The sheer volume is becoming another problem. VulnCheck data reveals there have been more than 100,000 software vulnerabilities logged this year by November 13, more than twice the total for 2025. Engineers still have to evaluate and deploy fixes without breaking the systems they are meant to protect.

That acceleration cuts both ways. Defenders gain powerful tools for finding weaknesses, but hasty repairs can disrupt essential systems, and malicious users may gain faster access to attack planning.

Hassan said she will make AI safety legislation an early priority when she takes over the Senate Homeland Security and Governmental Affairs Committee in January. She plans hearings examining risks to public safety and critical infrastructure; the GAO itself made no formal policy recommendations.

#5097. GTA VI Launch Sparks $250M Solana Stock Frenzy Over Nvidia

Home » Altcoins » Solana (SOL) » GTA VI Launch Sparks $250M Solana Stock Frenzy Over Nvidia

Tokenized Take-Two shares saw a massive 24-hour trading spike as on-chain markets attract Washington scrutiny.

The long-awaited release of Grand Theft Auto VI didn’t just break gaming records—it briefly turned publisher Take-Two Interactive into the hottest stock on the blockchain, outselling even AI giant Nvidia.

On November 18, decentralized exchange (DEX) trading volume for tokenized Take-Two shares (TTWO) exploded by 578%, jumping from $36.8 million to $249.5 million in a single day, according to Token Terminal data.

That was roughly 2.8 times the $88.1 million traded in tokenized Nvidia (NVDA) shares over the same 24-hour window, accounting for 42% of all on-chain stock trading.

Nearly all of the action, roughly $239 million, or 96% of total volume, happened on Solana via Backpack Securities, a subsidiary of crypto exchange Backpack. Launched earlier this year, the token is designed to be backed 1:1 by real Take-Two shares held in custody.

Traditional Wall Street traders saw a much calmer reaction: Take-Two’s Nasdaq shares rose 6.2% to close at $216.61 on launch day before pulling back 3.7% to $208.65 the next session.

Despite Take-Two’s one-day viral spike, Nvidia remains the undisputed heavyweight of tokenized equities, boasting a $399.4 million market cap and $2 billion in 30-day trading volume (compared to Take-Two’s $808.1 million).

Overall, tokenized public stocks on Solana have nearly doubled since late October to reach $1.22 billion.

That rapid growth is drawing fresh scrutiny in Washington. On November 16, four Democratic senators sent a letter to the Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) questioning how its newly introduced exemption for tokenized securities would address investor protection and anti-money laundering requirements.

The issue also surfaced during the November 19 Senate Banking Committee confirmation hearing for Kristin Johnson, President Donald Trump’s nominee for the SEC. Johnson indicated that exemptions covering tokenized stocks should eventually be formalized through rules following a public comment period.

The TTWO spike shows tokenized stocks can attract event-driven trading on-chain, comparable to some of the largest names in the segment. It also coincides with growing regulatory scrutiny of how tokenized stocks are supervised.

#5096. Ethereum’s staking case is strengthens while ETH’s price weakens – What’s going on?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Despite continued weakness across the broader crypto market, Ethereum [ETH] is showing an interesting development on the staking front.

According to Santiment’s data, Lido Staked Ethereum (stETH) recorded net outflows of 4,186 tokens from exchanges, marking its largest single-day outflow since the 30th of May.

As a result, there has been a decline in the amount of stETH immediately available on exchanges for potential selling.

This comes as Ethereum was trading near $1,753.91 at press time, after a drop of 2.9% in the past 24 hours.

At the same time, around 836,798 ETH is currently waiting to enter Ethereum’s validator network. Of this, the estimated waiting period currently stands at 14 days and 13 hours, with a processing rate (churn) of 256 ETH per epoch.

Meanwhile, the exit queue presents a different picture wherein around 164,005 ETH is waiting to leave the validator network, with an estimated waiting period of 3 days and 20 hours.

Looking at the broader picture, Ethereum currently has approximately 773,650 active validators, with 44.6 million ETH staked. This represents around 36.5% of Ethereum’s total supply, while the network’s annual percentage rate (APR) stands at 2.42%.

So now, if exchange outflows continue, the entry queue remains elevated, and ETH prices begin stabilizing, these developments could strengthen the argument that investors are positioning themselves for the longer term rather than preparing to sell into market weakness.

Interestingly, all of this coincided with a recent report by AMBCrypto where Ethereum’s staking ratio has reportedly reached an all-time high of 36.3%, with approximately 44.5 million ETH locked in staking, up from 42.1 million in mid-August.

Meanwhile, institutional players such as BitMine and SharpLink continue expanding their staking activities, with BitMine holding 5.22 million staked ETH and earning $121.4 million from staking and validation.

Geneva, Switzerland –  TRON DAO, the community-governed DAO dedicated to accelerating the decentralization of the internet through blockchain technology and decentralized applications (dApps), today announced that the Aave App, a high-yield savings mobile app developed and managed by Aave Labs, will add support for USDT (TRC-20) deposits on the TRON network as it expands to Android following its initial launch on iOS.

The launch combines Android’s global reach with TRON’s leading role in the stablecoin ecosystem, expanding mobile access to onchain savings opportunities that are not accessible on traditional savings apps. With support for USDT on TRON, eligible users in supported jurisdictions can now deposit USDT on TRON and start earning yield on their savings.

Android’s extensive global footprint offers a significant distribution opportunity for digital asset applications, particularly in markets where stablecoins are used for payments, remittances, and savings. TRON provides an established infrastructure for this opportunity, with more than $94 billion in circulating USDT, the largest circulating supply of Tether’s dollar-pegged stablecoin among blockchain networks. TRON also supports a broad range of stablecoin use cases, including payments, cross-border transfers, and digital asset transactions.

Aave is building the World’s Savings App, a simple, globally accessible tool designed to help people grow and manage their savings, with a delightful design experience on the surface and powerful onchain finance infrastructure working under the hood. Users of the app can earn yield on their savings, enjoy flexible access to their funds, and benefit from the Balance Protection program. They can also deposit and withdraw via stablecoins or banks in eligible countries and project their potential savings growth over the years with a future simulator. Following its availability on iOS, the Android launch expands the app’s mobile reach, while TRON support connects users to one of the largest USDT ecosystems in onchain finance.

“Aave Labs has a proven track record of building leading DeFi products, and the Aave App brings that expertise to everyday users,” said Justin Sun, Founder of TRON. “With TRON support on Android and iOS, more people can use the USDT they already hold on TRON to access simple, accessible onchain savings with the Aave App.”

“Millions of people in emerging economies already rely on stablecoins on TRON to save and send money,” said Stani Kulechov, Founder and CEO of Aave Labs. “The Aave App lets them earn on those dollars.”

By enabling USDT deposits through the Aave App on Android, the integration connects TRON’s established stablecoin ecosystem with a consumer-focused mobile application. The launch reflects the continued convergence of mobile applications and blockchain infrastructure, as consumer-focused products work to make digital assets and decentralized financial services more accessible worldwide.

TRON DAO is a community-governed DAO dedicated to accelerating the decentralization of the internet via blockchain technology and dApps.

Founded in September 2017, the TRON blockchain has experienced significant growth since its MainNet launch in May 2018. Today, TRON hosts the largest circulating supply of USD Tether (USDT) stablecoin, which currently exceeds $94 billion. As of November 2026, the TRON blockchain has recorded over 414 million in total user accounts, more than 16 billion in total transactions, and over $24 billion in total value locked (TVL), based on TRONSCAN. Recognized as the global settlement layer for stablecoin transactions and everyday purchases with proven success, TRON is “Moving Trillions, Empowering Billions.”

#5094. Bitcoin Trading Volume Surges to Highest Level Since February

Bitcoin (BTC), the world’s largest cryptocurrency, has recorded a multi-month high in daily trading volume as the cryptocurrency market remains in the grip of extreme volatility.

The massive spike in trading volume comes after Bitcoin tanked to two-year lows on Nov. 21 before bouncing back sharply to regain ground toward the psychologically crucial $60,000 level.

The massive spike in trading volume comes in the wake of one of the biggest sell-offs in BTC this year.

On Nov. 21, the world’s largest cryptocurrency dropped as low as around $55,100, a level last seen in September 2024. The sharp decline in Bitcoin prices came as risk assets tumbled amid rising concerns over the U.S. stock market and the artificial intelligence (AI) sector, which weighed on investor sentiment.

Following the sharp plunge, Bitcoin managed to make a stunning comeback as the flagship cryptocurrency recovered to trade back above $60,000 and briefly pushed toward the $61,000 mark.

The comeback, however, was short-lived as BTC lost ground again and tanked below the crucial $60,000 mark.

BTC is currently trading at around $58,879, rising 5.4% in the last 24 hours, as per the latest data from CoinGecko.

Furthermore, the world’s largest cryptocurrency has a 24-hour trading volume of around $43.8 billion and has regained a market cap of $1.18 trillion.

As per data from analytics firm CoinGlass, Bitcoin (BTC) futures trading volume stands at around $76.17 billion. On the other hand, the cryptocurrency's spot trading volume stands at $7.91 billion over the past 24 hours.

Despite the latest recovery, it is still trading 9.74% lower over the past seven days and more than 27% lower over the past month.

The world’s largest cryptocurrency is also down around 53.3% from its all-time high of $126,198.

Another massive wave of liquidations has swept through the cryptocurrency market following the latest recovery.

As per the latest data from CoinGlass, 85,533 traders have been liquidated in the last 24 hours, with total liquidations amounting to $562.12 million.

It is worth noting that around $406.13 million in liquidations were on the short side, while only $156 million were on the long side.

With regard to BTC, it alone accounts for around $346.18 million in liquidations, and a staggering 76.7% of those liquidations were on the bearish side.

At the moment, Bitcoin is unable to reclaim the $60,000 level with conviction.

DUBAI, UAE, November 18, 2026 – WSDT is introducing its prop trading platform for cryptocurrency perpetual futures, gold and tokenized US stocks. Traders can access these markets through a single simulated account, with one-step evaluations starting at a one-time fee of $19 for a $1,500 account.

The platform combines crypto perpetual futures, including Bitcoin and Ethereum, with xStocks instruments tracking companies such as Apple, NVIDIA and Tesla, alongside stock-index ETFs. Perpetual futures are contracts without an expiry date; xStocks track the prices of underlying shares or ETFs. On WSDT, these instruments are traded in a simulated environment, without ownership of the underlying shares or tokens.

Positions across the markets share the same account balance and count toward the same evaluation. Traders can work across crypto and equity-linked instruments in one terminal while managing a single set of account limits.

A WSDT challenge tests trading performance against a 10% profit target, a 4% daily loss limit and a 6% maximum drawdown fixed against the starting account size. Traders must trade on at least four separate days, with no deadline to complete the evaluation. Account sizes range from $1,500 to $200,000, with fees varying by size.

Traders who pass move to a funded-stage account, which is also simulated and retains the loss limits. They receive 80% of eligible profits under WSDT’s Provider Service Agreement. The evaluation fee pays for access to the challenge; it is not a trading deposit.

Registered users can open a free 14-day demo with a $100,000 simulated balance, without providing a payment card or making a deposit. The demo uses the same terminal as challenges, allowing traders to practice before entering an evaluation. Demo results do not qualify for payouts.

WSDT also runs an ongoing weekly competition with free entry. Every participant starts with a $240,000 simulated balance, and the top three finishers receive challenge accounts. A new round opens each Monday at noon New York time. Winners must pass their awarded challenge before progressing to the funded stage.

Registration, account options and full challenge rules are available on the WSDT website.

WSDT provides trader evaluation and educational services across crypto, gold and tokenized equity instruments. Built by TEFS, a prop trading firm established in 2017, it is operated by TEFS Marketing Management L.L.C. in Dubai, UAE. All WSDT trading accounts are simulated.

#5090. Anthony Scaramucci Predicts Bitcoin Bottom This Week

Bitcoin (BTC) bull and SkyBridge Capital founder Anthony Scaramucci has argued that the world's biggest cryptocurrency could find a critical bottom as soon as this week.

Scaramucci's comments follow the leading cryptocurrency's struggle to recover from a catastrophic crypto market crash that took BTC to its lowest price level in over two years.

In the latest episode of All Things Markets on Nov. 20, the SkyBridge Capital founder joined Galaxy Digital CEO Mike Novogratz to discuss a potential bottom for Bitcoin.

Scaramucci shared his view that Bitcoin could see its bottom on Nov. 25, citing the cryptocurrency's four-year halving cycle as the driving force behind his comments.

"Before we came on, I asked ChatGPT when's the expected halving for Bitcoin," Scaramucci explained.

"And then when are we 500 days out from that halving? And the estimate was November 25th," he added.

This prediction stems from the idea that Bitcoin's price trends could follow a predictable four-year cycle that coincides with the cryptocurrency's halving events, when the new BTC supply is cut in half.

This theory suggests that Bitcoin could find its major bottom around the 500-day mark before its next halving, expected in 2028.

However, these theories are by no means a certainty when it comes to predicting future price action, especially with the current level of institutional participation and macroeconomic forces impacting the crypto market to an unprecedented extent.

During the conversation, Novogratz also took note of Bitcoin's history of forming double bottoms during its four-year cycles.

"And every four years, Bitcoin double bottomed," Novogratz remarked.

At the time, the Galaxy Digital CEO's comments suggested a potentially positive scenario, as Bitcoin had been able to hold its psychological $60,000 price level.

"If it doesn't break 60, that could be a good sign," he noted.

Unfortunately for Bitcoin, the cryptocurrency subsequently crashed below this critical price point. This has left some wondering whether the cryptocurrency has already found its ultimate bottom.

During the same conversation, Novogratz suggested that Bitcoin's short-term outlook could largely depend on the current situation in the stock market.

Specifically, the billionaire said that a rebound in tech stocks could lead to a strong rally in Bitcoin's price.

"If we put in a bottom in the next couple days in the S&P here, down 3% from here, down 5% from here. And we have a rally back up. Man, Bitcoin is going to rally hard with it," Novogratz predicted.

However, he also warned that Bitcoin could see a lot of market-driven movements going forward, as opposed to crypto-specific movements.

"I think we're going to be stuck tethered to general sentiment and risk," he said.

Novogratz also believes that Bitcoin could trade in a range between $60,000 and $75,000 before a breakout or breakdown becomes apparent.

Bitcoin plunged to around $55,100 on Nov. 21, its lowest price level since September 2024, following another severe wave of sell-offs in the crypto market.

It then bounced back strongly to climb above $60,000 again, albeit briefly, before falling back to around $59,000 on Nov. 22.

#5089. Ledger Confirms Tampered Bitcoin Wallets in Berlin as Spy Implant Probe Spreads Beyond Asia

Ledger Confirms Tampered Bitcoin Wallets in Berlin as Spy Implant Probe Spreads Beyond Asia

The tampered Ledger wallets aren’t just a Southeast Asia problem anymore.

Ledger said Wednesday it has confirmed tampered hardware wallets in Berlin and is investigating reports of two more in Dubai, expanding a probe it had previously described as a localized incident in Southeast Asia.

“Our findings since then show tampered devices outside Southeast Asia. It is the same incident,” the company said in an update on its support site.

The investigation began last month after customers who bought devices from reseller CryptoBilis reported losses. Onchain investigators traced a suspected drain of about $87 million from hundreds of victim wallets across Ethereum, Tron, and Bitcoin.

Ledger said at the time that all confirmed cases were tied to CryptoBilis, and it later asked resellers in seven Asian markets to pull devices from sale.

Every device Ledger has confirmed shows the same kind of physical modification, designed to capture a user’s recovery phrase during setup. A recovery phrase, typically a string of 24 words, is the master key to a crypto wallet: anyone who has it can drain the funds.

Our investigation into the hardware implant incident we disclosed Oct 15th is ongoing, and we published an update: https://t.co/wx6mfQPr99

There is no flaw in Ledger's secure chip or operating system, and no confirmed case involving a device bought on https://t.co/vQ4QWa1D0f

Ledger maintains there’s no flaw in its secure chip or operating system and no confirmed case involving a device bought on ledger.com.

In Germany, Ledger said a few tampered units were localized to Berlin, and it urged anyone who bought a Ledger device there since January 2026 to contact support.

In Dubai, it’s working with distribution partners to learn how two suspected units entered circulation. Those devices were bought at a Virgin Megastore location, according to multiple reports. Ledger also found one affected Nano Gen5 among the Southeast Asian resellers it previously paused.

The company expects more tampered devices to surface, though it says the number remains small overall. Its Donjon security team is examining suspected devices, and Ledger has asked resellers to pause sales wherever inspections are ongoing.

Customers worried about a device should stop using it, Ledger said. It advises moving funds to a new wallet created on a device bought directly from ledger.com, without reusing the old recovery phrase or passphrase, since a tampered device can capture both. It also recommends filing a report with local authorities.

For a limited time, Ledger is offering physical inspections, including X-rays, at its factory in Vierzon, France.

“We will never ask for your recovery phrase,” the company said on X.

The last few months have been a rough stretch for hardware wallet markers overall. Rival manufacturer Trezor said this week that hackers who breached a third-party shipping partner likely took information tied to about 27,000 orders. That’s the company’s third such breach in as many months.

#5088. Dozens of Seats Go Unclaimed At Donald Trump’s Memecoin Dinner

Home » Altcoins » Dozens of Seats Go Unclaimed At Donald Trump’s Memecoin Dinner

Presently, only 130 crypto whale wallets qualified for Trump’s fancy memecoin dinner at the golf club.

Just 130 crypto wallets secured seats for the November 22 TRUMP token holder dinner at Donald Trump’s Virginia golf club, leaving dozens of the 185 available places unclaimed. The Official Trump token was trading near record lows at the time of the close.

The event is designed to reward the largest holders, with the top 29 wallets also eligible for a VIP reception. Contest participants held roughly $8.6 million in tokens combined when the qualification period ended, a sharp drop in engagement from the project’s first holder dinner.

BREAKING: 🇺🇸 Trump will host an exclusive dinner for the top 185 $TRUMP holders this Sunday.The coin is down 99% from its peak. pic.twitter.com/NxizWHlrVg

Ranking was based on time-weighted holdings, which encouraged accumulation and retention through the window.

More than 5,000 eligible holders reportedly did not register. The leaderboard’s combined value represented only a small fraction of TRUMP’s roughly $268 million market capitalization.

TRUMP was trading around $0.95 in the latest reporting, down roughly 46% over the prior month after setting a record low near $0.896 on November 14. The token remains well below its peak, with one account describing it as down almost 99% from its high.

Large holders added to positions during the dip. Nansen data showed the top 100 wallets increased their combined holdings by 1.27% over 30 days, while a separate whale cohort expanded holdings by 26.63%. Exchange balances fell slightly over the same period.

Top-ranked winners risk losing VIP benefits if balances drop below specified levels before the event, and lower-ranked winners can forfeit their seat by selling more than half their qualifying balance. Those restrictions expire once the dinner is over.

Ahead of the 2025 dinner, 92 of 220 invited holders reportedly sold their entire positions before the event.

A Super Bowl-themed leaderboard planned for February 2027 may give some buyers a reason to stay involved, but it does not remove the immediate question of whether winners will take liquidity afterward.

#5087. XRP Ledger Loses 60% of Users

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. XRP Ledger's network activity has dropped sharply, losing around 60% of the users it had yesterday. The decline comes amid a serious price correction and increased pressure on the cryptocurrency market. Selling pressure remains high, and the declining number of users is not helping the price.

XRPScan's data showed 250,000 users 24 hours ago, while today's reading shows barely 92,000 users. While the drop is significant, it should not be considered critical on its own. The decline partly reflects a regular weekend slowdown, when traders are generally less active, but it comes alongside the price correction and broader selling pressure.

The highest point for this metric in November was 300,000, with a monthly average of approximately 200,000 active users. The number of users is a critical metric for the network's health, and a decline toward 100,000 often comes before bullish sentiment among investors. The current drop does not guarantee that sentiment will turn, especially as the price remains under pressure.

Apart from the negative on-chain trend, XRP's price performance has also been weak. The price lost roughly 5% from its November 19 close and even fell below the $0.90 price level, which was expected to serve as a foundation for recovery. XRP was around $0.915 on November 22 after closing at $0.9604 three days earlier.

The trend remains bearish, and the decline could become significantly worse.

Few expected a surge in market liquidations so soon, as Bitcoin fell below the $60,000 price level. XRP and other altcoins followed the broader market trend and suffered serious losses.

Interest in the network appears to be weakening as the price correction continues, so this drop in activity is not helping XRP's recovery.

A potential recovery above $0.9 could be the key to a quick rebound. However, the overall trend remains bearish and is unlikely to change unless significant market events occur.

The report shows MEXC’s solid reserve position, with user assets in every major asset backed by reserves above 1:1, demonstrating MEXC’s ongoing commitment to safeguarding user assets.

Spanning Proof of Liabilities, Proof of Ownership, Reserves Calculation, and Merkle Tree verification, Hacken’s November 2026 audit confirms substantial over-collateralisation across core holdings, standing at 318% for BTC, 122% for USDT, 112% for ETH, and 110% for USDC. Guided by its user-first philosophy, MEXC continues to enhance transparency to protect user assets and build trust. #5085. Coinbase could claim a betting floor of their own, but what about Kalshi?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Coinbase wants a piece of the prediction markets action, with the exchange reportedly planning a launch next year.

Right now, they don’t run their own; they borrow them. Users trade event contracts on the exchange through a Kalshi partnership. That could change next year though.

Citing sources close to the matter, a Bloomberg report claimed that Coinbase wants to roll out its own event contracts in the U.S in H1 2027. It would be open to both retail and institutional traders.

But why bother? For starters, Coinbase gets to decide what launches and when. Next, it keeps a bigger share of the trading fees.

There’s real money at stake here. In Q3 2026, Coinbase’s prediction markets business was already on track to bring in over $200 million a year. If you think about it, this isn’t an overnight decision. In the run up to it all, Coinbase announced that it was buying prediction market startup The Clearing Company in December 2025.

Then, in September this year, the CFTC gave the green light for its own clearing house.

All that being said, nothing’s set in stone. However, the pieces are clearly falling into place.

Crypto trading may have slowed down, but prediction markets have kept themselves out of the bleeding.

Since launch, revenue from the business has grown by 82% QoQ on average. In Q3 alone, daily traders went up by over 60%, compared to the quarter before.

CFO Alesia Haas summed up the appeal pretty neatly. She said,

Prediction markets are adding another revenue stream and are less correlated to crypto volatility…

A big deal for an exchange that lives and dies by market cycles.

On the earnings call, Haas also said that the new clearing house would let Coinbase run its own contracts and earn more from them.

Worth pointing out, however, that Kalshi isn’t being cut out as partner contracts will stay on the platform.

#5084. Bitcoin Struggles to Hold $64,000 as ETF Outflows Hit $2.9B in November

Bitcoin Struggles to Hold $64,000 as ETF Outflows Hit $2.9B in November

Bitcoin is struggling to hold the $64,000 level as investors continue to pull money from spot Bitcoin ETFs and brace for another interest rate hike from the Federal Reserve.

The leading cryptocurrency was trading at around $63,800 on Thursday, down 1% on the day and 13% over the past week, according to Decrypt data. It has hovered between roughly $63,000 and $65,000 since the weekend, briefly dipping below $63,000 and touching a 24-hour low of $62,068, per CoinGecko data.

"Bitcoin remains under selling pressure," Daniela Hathorn, senior market analyst at Capital.com, told Decrypt, noting that it has fallen roughly 26% from its late-September peak near $87,000. "Elevated Treasury yields, dollar strength and expectations of further Fed tightening are weighing on risk appetite, while the broader technology selloff has added to the pressure."

U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded $306.4 million in net outflows on Wednesday, after $239.9 million on Tuesday, according to SoSoValue data. The funds have seen outflows on nine of the past 10 trading days, with Monday's small $8 million inflow the only exception, and have lost about $2.9 billion so far in November. Their total net assets have fallen to $77.7 billion. Bitcoin miners have added to the selling, with major miners including Riot and MARA selling some of their holdings to fund a shift into AI infrastructure.

Monday's brief inflow "does not signal a market inflection point," Tim Sun, senior researcher at HashKey, told Decrypt. Net short positions in Bitcoin futures held by leveraged funds on CME have fallen to their lowest level since early 2023, and futures basis yields have narrowed, he said, indicating that hedge funds have largely unwound the basis trade that paired spot ETF purchases with short futures. That means the current outflows are mainly driven by investors actively cutting their Bitcoin exposure, Sun said.

"If macro expectations do not improve, investors may shift from buying the dip to selling into rallies, creating additional overhead resistance on subsequent rebounds," he added.

"Recent Bitcoin ETF outflows and leveraged liquidations" have amplified the decline, Hathorn said. "Despite stronger Nvidia earnings, the market has struggled to stage a significant rebound, suggesting confidence remains fragile. A sustained recovery may require renewed ETF inflows and a more supportive interest-rate outlook."

Some traders have also pointed the finger at Anthropic's upcoming IPO for drawing money away from crypto, though analysts have disputed that the listing is draining crypto liquidity, attributing it instead to ETF outflows, forced selling and rising interest rates.

Traders see a 90.7% chance that the Fed will raise rates by a quarter point at its December 9 meeting, and a 9.3% chance of a half-point hike, according to CME FedWatch. The odds of a half-point move have risen from 6.5% a day earlier. Fed Governor Christopher Waller has called for an even bigger, half-point increase, unless policymakers signal another rise in January.

Hathorn said $64,000 is the immediate level to watch, followed by support around $62,000 and the psychological $60,000 mark. A sustained recovery above $66,000 to $68,000 could ease selling pressure, "but the broader trend remains bearish while lower highs persist," she said.

Onchain analytics firm Glassnode said in its weekly report on Tuesday that the crash had pushed investors’ realized losses to levels seen only near past market lows in 2018, 2020, 2022 and early 2023, a sign of capitulation. Bitcoin is now holding a dense block of recent buyers between $62,000 and $66,000, which grew by about 66,000 BTC during the two days of heaviest selling, Glassnode said. But a daily close below that block could set off a fast drop toward the June low of $58,500, and the Realized Price, the average price at which all Bitcoin last moved, at $53,700.

Traders on Decrypt Predict see a 43% chance that Bitcoin will fall to $60,000 at some point before the end of November, and a 12% chance that it will drop as low as $55,000. They give just a 2% chance that it will climb back to $82,500 this month.

There are some signs of support. About 1.1 million BTC, roughly 5.5% of supply, last changed hands at prices between $60,000 and $64,000, and that band has grown by more than 25,000 BTC in recent days, according to Neil Steinhardt, head of Nexo U.S., in a note shared with Decrypt Wednesday. A dense base of holders with costs near current prices "can act as a support zone, though it is not a floor," he wrote.

Bitcoin has also held up better than gold. The amount of gold one Bitcoin buys rose above its 200-day average this week, to about 17.1 ounces, around 40% above its March low, Steinhardt said.

"Shib.io is home to both Shibarium and ShibaSwap," Shiba Inu developer and long-time community member Mazrael said, citing the project’s public Git repository as evidence of the longstanding relationship.

Mazrael recently spoke about the link between the official web presence of the Shiba Inu ecosystem and its decentralized platforms in a post, stating that the information has been consistent since the launch of ShibaSwap.

@Heisenbergiqr3 @tacitfi @randhindi @LCSHIB @Shibtoken https://t.co/RP8CH8r1zW is home of Shibarium and Shibaswap as indicated in our public git that has not changed since Shibaswap and is the same git for shibarium

He pointed to the project’s public Git as proof that Shib.io is the home of Shibarium and ShibaSwap.

The clarification strengthens the link among Shib.io, ShibaSwap and Shibarium, two key components of the Shiba Inu ecosystem.

The broader crypto market is seeing a short-term rebound, but it does not fully establish that the decline has ended. According to Santiment, “Stablecoin Whale Dry Powder Continues Shrinking Across Crypto.”

The largest tracked stablecoin whales are holding far less dry powder than before, Santiment stated, noting that since the start of the year, USDT balances on Ethereum held by the top 100 whales have fallen by $15.14 billion (-21.1%) and USDC (on Ethereum) balances are down $6.72 billion (-23.7%).

Stablecoins are crypto’s ready-to-deploy cash, so large whale balances can represent potential buying power for BTC and altcoins. Falling balances can mean funds were deployed, redeemed, transferred elsewhere, or moved outside these tracked wallets. Either way, having less USDT and USDC concentrated among major holders means less obvious dry powder waiting on the sidelines.

#5082. Ripple ETF Investors Hold On, But XRP Buyers Go Missing

Home » Trendsetter News » Ripple ETF Investors Hold On, But XRP Buyers Go Missing

XRP ETF assets fell 31.6% despite modest redemptions, suggesting a lack of fresh demand, not a buyer exodus.

Ripple’s XRP-linked ETF assets fell 31.6% from September 30 through the Friday cited in the video, compared with a 20.2% decline for Bitcoin funds. Yet redemptions remained modest.

Dana Love, PhD, a popular market analyst examining ETF flows and corporate filings, argues that missing buyers, not an investor stampede, help explain the XRP’s deeper slide this month.

The XRP coin traded at $0.967 this Friday morning, following a last Friday low of $0.89.

Dana Love’s central claim: Bitcoin-related selling is weighing on XRP, while demand from XRP funds and a closely watched treasury company has failed to provide much support.

According to the figures presented, cumulative net inflows into XRP ETFs slipped from $1.809 billion on October 29 to $1.789 billion on Monday. That leaves roughly 98.9% of cumulative net inflows intact, despite the sharp decline in fund assets.

But November through that Monday brought no positive daily net flows. “Nobody is running for the exits,” the analyst says, “but nobody new was walking in.”

Bitcoin ETFs, meanwhile, recorded roughly $2.34 billion in November outflows through the same period. Monday’s $8 million inflow interrupted the selling, although the analyst cautions that one positive session does not establish a reversal.

The distinction matters: limited XRP ETF redemptions do not establish that XRP holders across the entire market are refusing to sell. Nor does a decline in fund assets measure withdrawals alone; falling token prices also reduce those balances.

Dana Love also questions Evernorth, the XRP treasury company whose October 14 release said it would “accumulate and deploy” XRP into institutional strategies beginning in the fourth quarter of 2026.

The video estimates that Evernorth had about $111 million in cash, including subsequent warrant proceeds, and says its shares still traded above estimated underlying asset value.

That could support further fundraising, although the company had not announced additional purchases in the period discussed.

Silence is not proof of inactivity. The analyst explicitly acknowledges that Evernorth may be buying without announcing every transaction.

For investors, the useful distinction is between broader market relief and renewed XRP-specific market demand.

Sustained Bitcoin ETF inflows would suggest selling pressure is easing; XRP inflows arriving independently, or a disclosed Evernorth purchase, would offer stronger evidence that XRP has found buyers of its own.
