#5081. OmenX announces $2 million in funding from Informed Ventures and launches on-demand 30-second Bitcoin predictions

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Toronto, November 19, 2026 – OmenX, a Base-native leveraged prediction market platform, today announced $2 million in funding from Informed Ventures alongside the launch of its on-demand, 30-second Bitcoin price prediction product.

The new product lets users predict whether Bitcoin’s price will rise or fall over the next 30 seconds, with each trade starting its own prediction window. A position opened at 10:00:12 runs until 10:00:42. Users can act when their view forms, with a full 30-second horizon, rather than adapting to the remaining time in a shared market round.

Featuring zero-slippage execution and rapid settlement, the product extends the familiar five- and fifteen-minute crypto prediction format to a shorter, individually timed experience. It adds to OmenX’s intraday offering, which spans prediction horizons from 30 seconds to 24 hours, depending on the market.

Beyond crypto, OmenX offers directional prediction markets linked to more than 100 global assets, including U.S. equities, futures, ETFs and indices. Its event markets also cover live sports and esports, with sports coverage spanning major football leagues, basketball, American football, ice hockey, baseball and UFC.

Leverage has been central to OmenX’s platform from the outset, allowing traders to amplify their exposure on supported event markets. The latest launch builds on that foundation while advancing a broader goal: making prediction markets a practical financial tool for retail users.

OmenX organizes the trading decision around a defined question: will an asset’s price be higher or lower at the end of a specified period? Users can express a directional view without purchasing the underlying asset, targeting an exact closing price or constructing an options position that requires decisions about strikes, implied volatility and time decay.

“Leverage has been part of OmenX from the start, but our ambition goes beyond adding leverage to prediction markets,” said James, founder and CEO of OmenX. “We want to make global markets easier to participate in. A trader should be able to take a view on Bitcoin, a stock or a sporting event and express it through a clear contract and a timeframe that fits their judgment.”

The platform’s emphasis on short, recurring markets provides faster feedback on trading decisions and can reduce the time capital remains committed to individual outcomes. OmenX plans to expand its coverage of traditional and crypto assets while deepening the trading experience across supported markets.

OmenX is a Base-native leveraged prediction market platform covering crypto, global financial assets, live sports and esports. Through defined outcomes, short settlement cycles and leverage on supported markets, OmenX is building a more accessible way to trade market views.

Learn more at omenx.com. Media contact James Zhang

#5080. Best Hyperliquid trading bots: AI agents and bots compared

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Hyperliquid has become one of the busiest venues for crypto perpetual futures, and a long list of tools now automate trading on it. They differ less in what they promise than in two practical questions: who decides the trades, and what the tool is allowed to do with your account. This guide compares the main Hyperliquid trading bots and AI trading agents in 2026, plus Hyperliquid’s vaults, against those questions. Each tool was compared on who decides the trades, what its key can do with your account, and what evidence of performance exists, using each provider’s own documentation in October 2026.

Most Hyperliquid tools connect through an API wallet, which Hyperliquid also calls an agent wallet. The account owner approves a separate key that can sign orders for the account but cannot withdraw or transfer funds. These approvals expire, and approving a new wallet under the same name replaces the old one. Some tools also recommend a sub-account, which keeps the bot’s balances and positions separate. The assets stay in the user’s own Hyperliquid account throughout, which is why most Hyperliquid bots can fairly be called non-custodial.

Many tools charge through a builder fee, a small fee added to each filled order. It applies only after the user approves a maximum fee from the main wallet, and Hyperliquid caps it at 0.1% on perpetuals.

Tools are grouped by who supplies the trading logic: a managed model first, then signal execution, rule-based bots and open-source software (3Commas sits with the rule-based bots). Vaults come last because they replace a bot rather than run one. Hyperliquid’s own trading and funding fees apply to every option.

AlphaNet is built for Hyperliquid and supplies the trading logic itself. Users pick one of its deep learning strategies for crypto perpetual futures and an allocation, and the model decides when to enter and exit each trade. It connects the way the exchange intends: the user approves an agent wallet that can place and cancel orders but cannot withdraw or transfer, and trades run from the user’s own account. New deployments allow leverage up to 3× account equity. On cost, AlphaNet’s Hyperliquid trading bot charges no platform fee during the current promotional period, compared with builder fees of 0.035–0.1% per fill on several bots below; Hyperliquid’s protocol fees still apply, and a dynamic trading fee applies afterwards.

A newer group of AI agent projects on Hyperliquid connect language models to an API wallet and let them place trades. They are mostly early. In the first season of Alpha Arena, a short real-money competition on Hyperliquid, four of the six language models that took part finished with losses and two with gains.

WunderTrading is built for traders whose strategy already exists as TradingView alerts or external signals. It turns those alerts into Hyperliquid orders and also offers DCA and grid bots, charging a 0.035% builder fee with no separate subscription.

goodcryptoX is a no-code app for grid, DCA, trailing, and TradingView bots on iOS, Android, and the web. Gainium covers similar bot types and adds backtesting and paper trading, with a choice between a free mode that adds a builder fee and paid plans that do not. Bitsgap brings its hosted grid and DCA bots to Hyperliquid alongside the centralised exchanges it already supports, and 3Commas, one of the most widely used bot platforms, lists Hyperliquid among its supported exchanges for spot and futures. With all of them, the bot executes your logic; the tool does not supply a trading edge.

Hummingbot has Hyperliquid spot and perpetual connectors and includes market-making scripts and arbitrage, TWAP, and grid executors. The self-run client charges no builder fee. It accepts either an API wallet or the main wallet’s private key; the second gives the software full control of the account, so the API wallet is the safer choice.

Rule-based bots have no platform-level record, because results depend on each user’s settings. Vaults are the most transparent option: every vault’s history is on-chain and visible in Hyperliquid’s app. Managed strategy platforms sit in between. AlphaNet publishes a running record of each strategy since its start date, and because trades run from users’ own accounts, each user’s fills are on-chain and can be checked against it.

Instead of connecting a tool to your own account, you deposit into a vault that a leader trades. The protocol’s own vault, HLP, provides liquidity, performs liquidations, and earns a share of fees. User-run vaults follow individual strategies. Leaders take 10% of profits and must keep at least 5% of the vault, and deposits are locked for one day (four days for HLP).

The choice comes down to the two questions this guide started with. First, decide who you want making the trades: your own rules, a signal you already trust, a trained model, or a vault leader. Then check what the tool is allowed to do with your account. On Hyperliquid, most tools need only an agent wallet that can trade but not withdraw, so there is rarely a reason to hand over a main wallet key or move funds into someone else’s control.

Two further checks are worth making before committing real capital. The first is cost per fill: builder fees on top of Hyperliquid’s own fees can add up. The second is the record: a vault’s history is on-chain, a managed strategy should publish a live one, and a rule-based bot has none until you build your own.

#5079. Ethereum Drafts New Gas Rules for Validator Deposits

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Ethereum developers want to change the gas accounting for validator deposits. The plan is to measure the request bytes that these deposit events create.

The gas calculation already accounts for executing a deposit. However, after the deposit is finished, a 192-byte request is passed along with the block. Those 192 bytes are excluded from the minimum gas charge, so the same gas limit might allow more data than the initial calculation accounts for.

Under Gloas, there is no fixed cap on the number of deposit requests per block. The execution layer has not capped this either. Gas is the only limiting factor.

Neither EIP-8131 nor EIP-8279 accounts for those request bytes. This means that deposits could become another way to bypass the gas floor once EIP-8279 closes the loophole involving cold storage reads.

A relay contract can call the deposit contract repeatedly, costing around 26,000 gas per deposit. In the author's example, that execution cost is below the point where the gas floor for zero-byte calldata would start to increase the total charge.

This results in a payload of about 1.44 times the block gas limit divided by 64, compared with about 1.15 times the same baseline.

The main issue is the data size, so the proposal directly addresses the data.

A log from the deposit contract with the deposit event topic counts as 192 bytes before the log entry is added. This is the same condition EIP-6110 uses to turn logs into requests.

The cost of this would be another check that client developers would have to implement and test.

Users are not expected to pay more for regular deposits. The cost of executing a regular deposit is already above the proposed minimum, so the extra 12,288 gas only raises the minimum charge.

The proposal has yet to be scheduled for a network upgrade.

#5078. GameStop CEO Says Digital-Only Games Have ‘Hurt Consumers’ as GTA 6 Skips Discs

GameStop CEO Says Digital-Only Games Have ‘Hurt Consumers’ as GTA 6 Skips Discs

GameStop CEO Ryan Cohen has criticized video game publishers for abandoning physical discs, as Grand Theft Auto 6, one of the most anticipated games ever made, launches on Thursday as a digital download only.

"When you buy a game, you should be able to sell it, trade it or lend it to a friend," Cohen told Bloomberg. "Unquestionably, the decision to move to all digital has hurt consumers, and it's also hurt GameStop."

Cohen declined to name specific companies, but Take-Two Interactive is releasing the $80 game only as a download, with retailers selling boxes containing download codes. Asked about presales, Cohen said: "It's a code in a box. I mean, we're selling a lot of it."

Take-Two declined to comment to Bloomberg.

The shift hits a core part of GameStop's business. Pre-owned and refurbished products made up 22% of the retailer's sales, about $171 million, in the quarter that ended August 1, down 32% from a year earlier, according to Bloomberg.

Cohen said he has spoken with publishers, who he argued benefit from limiting trade-ins because used copies compete with new sales. "They're taking away competition," he said.

Sony announced in July that it will stop producing physical discs for new PlayStation games in January 2028, a move experts have said will hit low-income gamers hardest. PlayStation founder Ken Kutaragi has dismissed the backlash as a "mentality issue," while an Infinity Ward veteran said publishers have pushed to cut discs since 2007.

Cohen said he has met with Rep. Ritchie Torres (D-NY), who is backing the Power to the Players Act, which would require gaming companies to give consumers ongoing access to the titles they buy. "A game that can be taken away the moment a company pulls the plug is not a purchase. It is a rental disguised as a sale," Torres said in October.

In Europe, the Stop Killing Games campaign gathered 1.3 million signatures for a European Citizens' Initiative calling for publishers to keep games playable after support ends.

The question of whether buyers own digital games has also reached the courts. Sony has argued in court that digital games can't be owned, prompting some in the crypto industry to pitch blockchain-based ownership as a fix.

Cohen's GameStop has also made bets beyond gaming. The company bought 4,710 BTC for more than $500 million in 2025, and in March moved all but one of them into a covered call strategy on Coinbase Prime to generate income. In May, after eBay rejected its takeover offer, GameStop sought shareholder approval to raise its authorized share count to 2.5 billion as it built a stake in the online marketplace.

#5075. Solana’s 2027 roadmap promises faster growth, but the market wants proof

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Solana’s core developer, Anza, has unveiled its 2027 roadmap.

Structurally, the roadmap focuses on making Solana faster, more efficient, and capable of handling more activity. The network aims to double its compute capacity and cut the compute units needed for token account transactions by 80%.

It also plans to increase the number of accounts supported in each transaction from 64 to 96 and make it easier to run models fully on-chain.

Naturally, the question becomes whether Solana is preparing for its next growth phase. Interestingly, the previous major network upgrade was also performed by Anza.

This upgrade, called Alpenglow, brought down Solana’s finality time to 150ms from 12.8 seconds, making the network significantly faster.

The result? As the chart above shows, Solana processed over 10 billion transactions in October, keeping monthly activity above that level. However, more than halfway through November, the network has recorded just over 2 billion transactions.

That’s 5x lower than October’s total, suggesting that the recent spike in activity may be losing steam. Given this, it might be too early to expect any bull run in 2027.

In fact, SOL is currently down 20% in October, which indicates that the market hasn’t seen increased demand from improving fundamentals just yet.

So, what does it say about the recent “Anthropic-driven” momentum on Solana [SOL]?

SPCX was first to demonstrate how major IPOs can bring fresh trading activity to Solana.

The launch of SpaceX’s tokenized stock, SPCX, on Solana on the 12th of June brought significant activity to the network.

The token recorded $108 million in 24-hour on-chain trading volume shortly after launch and crossed 10,000 holders within six days, with cumulative trading volume exceeding $350 million.

Notably, a similar trend has emerged following the ANT IPO launch. As the chart below shows, Anthropic’s ANT has generated over $29 million in trading volume on Solana since launch across three issuers. Backpack’s token alone accounted for $26.8 million, or nearly 92% of the total.

This shows how major IPO launches are bringing fresh trading activity to Solana.

Adding to this, DeFiLlama data shows that Solana’s stablecoin market cap has risen nearly 3% in the past 24 hours, pointing to a fresh liquidity inflow after months of weakness.

However, the bigger picture remains mixed.

Despite the rise in on-chain activity and liquidity, SOL is still trading around $70. This suggests that the recent activity has yet to translate into stronger buying pressure.

For Solana’s 2027 roadmap to support a longer-term recovery, the network will therefore need to sustain this growth beyond short-term activity.

Anza’s 2027 roadmap aims to improve Solana’s speed, capacity, and overall network efficiency.

SPCX and ANT have boosted on-chain trading activity, but SOL’s price weakness suggests that stronger demand is still missing.

Big-time speed performance makes Solana edge the apex crypto asset, but both are in losing key thresholds.

$SOL Starts to Outperform Bitcoin pic.twitter.com/qQ8Lgrfmgr

On the shorter-term chart, SOL is attempting to stabilize after a sharp decline, with analysts watching for a potential wave completion that could open room for a bounce if local support holds.

#5071. 30K Bitcoin and A LOT of noise — It’s what a Tesla-SpaceX merger really means for crypto

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

“Do I think it would be a good idea? Yes.”

That’s what Elon Musk said about the idea to merge Tesla and SpaceX, back in October 2026.

Since then, however, it would seem that this somewhat fanciful idea has only accelerated. Over the last few weeks alone, both Tesla and SpaceX have constituted special exploratory committees consisting of independent directors.

Will it go through? Probably, yes. After all, despite the CEO of SpaceX and Tesla claiming that he is a “conflicted party,” his companies’ shareholders have often acceded to his wishes. That’s an opinion Walter Isaacson, author of “Elon Musk,” shares too.

Especially after the entrepreneur made his case by stating,

…there’s a lot of merit to combining the companies as we are pretty much in the Super Intelligence singularity.

Needless to say, such a merger will have some legal, regulatory, and financial implications. But what about the crypto sector?

Tesla, Inc., the automotive and clean energy company, holds 11,509 BTC valued at over $747 million right now. Similarly, SpaceX, the aerospace manufacturer, holds 18,712 BTC worth over $1.22 billion.

In simple terms, the merger will simply be a transaction that pools in digital assets owned by both these entities into a single balance sheet. This consolidation would allow the merged entity to hold 30,221 BTC in reserves.

Those are significant numbers. However, here’s where it’s also worth looking at the context and scale and the bigger picture.

Neither Tesla nor SpaceX are crypto companies. They are primarily tech manufacturing companies with valuations of $1.54 trillion and $1.89 trillion, respectively.

Yes, they have crypto holdings, but they aren’t significant enough for each individual entity to suggest that these are more than a passing interest and a diversification opportunity.

To put it in financial terms, for both Tesla and SpaceX, Bitcoin is a non-core balance sheet asset. For them, their BTC holdings are financially immaterial to their core CapEx. As Dr. Andrei Belonogov, Managing Partner at TechAccountingPro pointed out,

The size of treasuries is relatively small compared to the company’s debt maturities and non-cancellable commitments.

This will remain the case if the merger does go through. If and when it happens, the merged companies’ valuation would dwarf the value of its crypto holdings.

What about after, though? This is an important question because more often than not, non-core balance sheet assets go through a period of evaluation and restructuring and sometimes, disposal too to streamline operations and facilitate profitability.

Could this happen to the aforementioned Bitcoin holdings then? Well, that’s always a possibility. However, recent history suggests the odds are low.

Consider the purchase and sale history of both these companies, for instance. The last time Tesla sold any of its Bitcoin holdings was back in 2022. Since then, it has bought additional BTC while also holding on to the stash it already had.

Similarly, SpaceX last sold any of its holdings back in 2024. In the two years since, the aerospace manufacturer has only bought Bitcoin. Specifically, over 10K BTC in May 2026.

Now, it’s up for debate how much input the “conflicted party,” Musk, had in any of these sales or purchases. However, it is safe to assume that, given the consistencies between the two boardrooms so far, that will remain the case after the merger too.

What about later though? Well, MetaWin COO Rory Cartwright told AMBCrypto,

The merger doesn’t necessarily mean a sell-off or any spike in price. What it will do, however, is put a roughly $2bn position into a single company’s balance sheet. Bitcoin can be especially volatile, so that may make some investors seasick

Will the merged entity enjoy the same degree of flexibility Tesla and SpaceX enjoyed individually when it comes to crypto sales and purchases though? According to Kevin Constantine, Senior Associate at Caldwell Law,

Protections could become relevant to cryptocurrency holdings, particularly if management’s ability to purchase, liquidate, or transfer Bitcoin raised concerns about the companies’ valuations or the treatment of shareholders.

The fact that both Tesla and SpaceX refused to sell their Bitcoin in 2026, a year that has been particularly bearish for the crypto-sector, is testament to the confidence they have in this asset. It is also a sign of institutional conviction.

After the merger, the merged entity will have the potential to be the sixth-largest corporate Bitcoin holder in the world. Only behind crypto-native firms or treasury-focused players like MicroStrategy and Metaplanet. All while Bitcoin remains its non-core balance sheet asset.

That, more than anything, will be confirmation of Bitcoin’s value as a corporate treasury asset. That might not sound like much on paper, but that is still VERY bullish news.

What could be even better though? According to Chris Mack, CTO at Quote.Trade,

If Musk came out publicly bullish on Bitcoin and crypto, that would change things. Investors would start paying attention to whether he plans to buy more or make crypto part of the business.

That’s not all either. As it stands, both Tesla and SpaceX have significant investments in scaling AI infrastructure. This is likely to continue after the merger, with more capital and resources allocated to AI compute and energy grids.

In a very indirect way, such investments could have a positive impact on AI-focused crypto tokens like RENDER, TAO, and NEAR, especially in the long run.

At the time of writing, reports seemed to suggest that Tesla and SpaceX are targeting a potential deal announcement by January 2027 or even as early as December 2026.

That’s an accelerated timeline, one that may or may not have anything to do with Democrats’ victory in the midterm elections. Democrats, many of whom are also planning to probe Elon Musk’s corporate ties.

Even outside the political element, though, it probably won’t be all sunshine and roses. After all, this is a merger between a major U.S. defense and Starlink contractor with an automative company with a significant manufacturing and sales footprint in China.

The regulatory and legal questions involved alone will be a nightmare.

Will crypto be an irrelevant factor then? Speaking to AMBCrypto, Constantine claimed,

As it stands, a lot of things are still up in the air. It’s too early to say how Tesla and SpaceX will quell national security concerns while juggling regulatory challenges with this merger. That’s the unpredictable part.

What is not unpredictable is what will happen to their cryptos and the crypto sector at large if this merger goes through. Relatively speaking, that is.

#5070. Binance Wins $6.5B Freezing Order Over Hack That Can Be Served via the Blockchain

Binance Wins $6.5B Freezing Order Over Hack That Can Be Served via the Blockchain

Binance has secured a $6.5 billion worldwide freezing order against the hackers behind last month's attack on the exchange, which the FBI has blamed on North Korea.

The injunction was issued by the courts of the Abu Dhabi Global Market (ADGM) against the unknown perpetrators, Binance's lawyers at Gibson Dunn confirmed, according to City AM.

The order breaks new legal ground. It can be served directly via the blockchain, and market participants will be notified of a "living list" of 26,000 wallet addresses identified as holding stolen assets, Gibson Dunn partner Kendall Day said. That will enable "those assets to be frozen rapidly even as they are laundered at pace," Day said.

Binance has also secured proprietary injunctions and delivery-up orders, including against parties holding frozen assets who are not accused of wrongdoing, according to City AM. Decrypt has reached out to Gibson Dunn for comment.

"We will continue using every lawful tool available to trace, freeze and recover stolen assets, support our partners in returning funds where possible, and keep those assets out of the hands of the attackers," Dugan Bliss, Binance's deputy general counsel, said in a statement reported by Bloomberg.

The FBI has attributed the October 29 hack to TraderTraitor, a group linked to North Korea, which has denied involvement. The attackers gained access after an engineer was phished through a fake job offer, according to Bloomberg. The Secret Service has joined the FBI's investigation.

About $2.85 billion in funds connected to the hack has been frozen, including by Ripple and World Liberty Financial, according to Bloomberg. Stablecoin issuers Tether and Circle froze $2.7 billion shortly after the attack, and the Justice Department said last week it had obtained court authorization to seize or restrain $1.2 billion of that.

The hackers have used thousands of wallets and multiple blockchains to launder the rest, and wallets linked to the hack began moving Ethereum again this week after two weeks of inactivity.

Binance has said it fully covered the $6.3 billion in customer assets affected and absorbed the losses itself.

#5069. Hoskinson Weighs In on Cardano (ADA) Price Collapse

Charles Hoskinson, founder of Cardano (ADA), has tried to clear the air regarding the utility of the cryptocurrency. This comes as ADA has fallen more than 95% from its all-time high.

Cardano founder Charles Hoskinson responded to frustrated Cardano community members who are disappointed by the blockchain’s pace of development and inability to rebound from the current slump.

On Nov. 21, Cardano founder Charles Hoskinson took to X to counter criticism about the current state of Cardano.

The exchange was triggered by a joke made by an X user stating that Grand Theft Auto 6 came out before a working Cardano blockchain.

Hoskinson replied to the comment, saying: “Cardano works.”

Later, Hoskinson addressed the poor price performance of the cryptocurrency and explained that price doesn’t always reflect the capabilities of blockchain networks.

“Yeah. Price doesn't equal utility. Is Solana 75 percent less capable than it's all time high? It's an infinitely better product today for many more people. And so it is for Cardano,” he said.

What this means is that even if the value of a blockchain network's native cryptocurrency is dropping, the technology can still advance.

An X user, however, contested his comparison with Solana, stating that Cardano hasn’t been able to retake its previous all-time high even after years of development.

This drew an increasingly irritated response from Hoskinson.

“So every single thing a f****** scam? Everything they do failed? I’m so tired of you people,” said the Cardano founder.

This recent statement by Hoskinson comes as Cardano (ADA) remains one of the worst-performing major cryptocurrencies in the current market downturn.

As per CoinGlass data, ADA is currently trading at around $0.14. The cryptocurrency is down by about 57% since the beginning of 2026.

It has lost a whopping 95.4% of its value from its all-time high of $3.09, set in September 2021.

On Nov. 21, ADA briefly dipped toward $0.13. This came as another sell-off hit the broader crypto market.

Cardano has been in a steady decline, leading to criticism regarding its adoption, activity in its ecosystem and ability to compete with the likes of Ethereum and Solana.

Nonetheless, Charles Hoskinson has maintained that short-term market performance should not be conflated with the progress of blockchain technology over the long run.

Earlier this month, the Cardano founder tried to reassure frustrated ADA holders and tell them that things would eventually get better.

“It's all going to be ok. Everyone will be happy next year after all this madness, hate and idiocy,” said Hoskinson in a previous X post.

#5068. Banks Are Adopting Stablecoins: Here Is Why Crypto-Native Dollars Still Win on User Freedom

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

As traditional giants rush into digital dollars, USDD’s Yvonne Chia explains why 24/7 access, self-custody, and transparent yield give decentralized stablecoins an edge banks can’t replicate.

20 November – Road Town, Totorla, British Virgin Islands – Decentralized stablecoin protocol USDD is accelerating its multichain utility across TRON, Ethereum, and BNB Chain—combining automated sUSDD yield via its Smart Allocator with GasFree zero-TRX transfers—as traditional financial giants race to build competing stablecoin rails.

In a recent industry interview with instant exchange partner ChangeNOW, USDD Head of Communications Yvonne Chia outlined why self-custody, 24/7 settlement, and transparent onchain reserves give decentralized digital dollars a structural advantage over bank-issued alternatives.

For billions of people globally, opening and maintaining a traditional bank account still involves paperwork, minimum balance requirements, geographic restrictions, and approval delays. Even when banks integrate stablecoin rails behind the scenes, those services remain gated behind legacy compliance walls and intermediary control.

Crypto-native stablecoins flip that model on its head. As Yvonne Chia highlighted during the interview:

“Compare this to a stablecoin like USDD, where all you need is a phone, a working internet connection, you can immediately start a wallet and you have access to your funds right away.”

With USDD, users can access and manage their digital dollars directly through their own wallets, without depending on traditional financial intermediaries.

This represents a fundamental shift in financial access: users are no longer limited to the services, operating hours, or account structures of a single institution. Instead, they have the freedom to hold, manage, and transfer their assets on their own terms.

Self-custody also gives users greater autonomy over their financial decisions. They can choose which wallets to use, where to transfer their assets, and which supported decentralized applications to interact with, without needing to route every transaction through a centralized financial institution.

With USDD, users’ digital dollars can move with them across an open blockchain ecosystem, rather than remaining tied to a particular bank or service provider.

Legacy payment networks still operate on decades-old schedules: weekend cutoffs, bank holidays, and multi-day cross-border settlement delays accompanied by hefty wire fees.

Blockchain networks never close. Whether it is a Sunday night or a public holiday, USDD moves globally without depending on traditional banking operating hours or settlement windows.

“We are able to have transfers 24/7 and we’re not limited to any traditional banking hours. So I think that in some ways, stablecoins are not just becoming banks, but exceeding what traditional banks can offer to users.”

To make everyday transfers even smoother than fintech apps, USDD introduced GasFree integration on TRON, allowing users on supported wallets to pay network fees directly in USDD without needing to hold TRX.

This removes another practical barrier to stablecoin adoption: having to acquire and maintain a separate gas token just to transfer digital dollars. With GasFree, eligible users can pay approximately 0.5 USDD per transaction, compared with around 1.5 USDT for a standard USDT transfer under the referenced fee conditions.

These improvements matter because the usefulness of a stablecoin is not determined by its peg alone. Low-cost transfers, straightforward wallet experiences, and around-the-clock availability are what make digital dollars practical for everyday payments and global value transfers.

Perhaps the sharpest contrast between banks and decentralized stablecoins lies in how yield is distributed.

In traditional banking, institutions take customer deposits, deploy them into yield-generating markets, and retain a significant portion of the returns. Everyday depositors may receive relatively low interest rates, while higher-yielding products often come with eligibility requirements, minimum deposits, or fixed maturity periods.

Stablecoins introduce another way to participate in digital-dollar opportunities. Rather than keeping capital entirely within a traditional deposit account, users can access onchain yield opportunities through decentralized protocols and transparent investment mechanisms.

USDD was built to return that value directly to holders. Through USDD Earn, users can deposit USDD to receive sUSDD, earning automated, competitive yield powered by our Smart Allocator system, with no fixed lock-up periods restricting access to their capital.

For users, the distinction is important: USDD Earn provides access to a productive stablecoin while maintaining flexibility, rather than requiring users to commit their capital to a conventional fixed-term product.

Traditional banks ask customers to trust closed-door balance sheets and periodic regulatory filings—until a crisis proves otherwise.

USDD takes a different approach through its “Don’t trust, verify” ethos, emphasizing publicly accessible onchain information and independent verification.

Transparency does not eliminate financial or technical risks, but it changes how users can evaluate them. Instead of relying exclusively on an institution’s assurances, users have access to information that can be independently examined and verified.

The bigger picture is an interconnected financial ecosystem in which users can move between holding, transferring, exchanging, and deploying their stablecoins according to their own needs. These are not isolated functions. Together, they make stablecoins more useful as everyday financial infrastructure.

Banks will undoubtedly continue using stablecoin rails to modernize their back-end settlement. However, true financial freedom comes from open networks where assets flow freely across protocols without intermediaries.

For USDD, the opportunity goes beyond offering a dollar-pegged asset. It is about making digital dollars accessible, transferable, and productive across an interconnected financial ecosystem. From self-custody and 24/7 transfers to flexible yield opportunities and multichain DeFi access, these capabilities give users more ways to put their money to work.

Banks may be bringing stablecoins into traditional finance. Crypto-native protocols are showing what stablecoins can make possible beyond it.

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

XRP has lost 25.4% so far in November 2026, according to CryptoRank. Before this year, the token's biggest November loss was 23.3% in 2019, according to CryptoRank data, and the token lost another 16.8% in December 2019. On Nov. 22, XRP traded near $0.90, according to TradingView.

The token also lost 10% and 11.8% in November of 2021 and 2022, respectively. In November of 2023 and 2024, XRP gained 1.4% and 281.7%.

In November 2018, the crypto market crashed after the Bitcoin Cash hard fork, and XRP lost 19.3% that month. In December 2018, the token fell 2.25%. Two years earlier, XRP dropped 18% in November 2016 and 4.07% in December 2016.

XRP's median December return is -4.07%, and its average December return is +63.1%, according to CryptoRank data. Most of that average comes from December 2017, when XRP gained 818.9%.

XRP has closed December in the red in eight of the past 13 years. In December of 2021, 2022 and 2025, the token lost 17%, 16.9% and 14.8%, respectively, and in December 2024, it gained 6.94%.

In 2020, XRP's December drop came as the U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission sued Ripple on Dec. 22, 2020. The token lost 66.5% that month, and in January 2021, it gained 122.1%.

In 2025, XRP's December drop came after the token set its all-time high of $3.65 on July 18, 2025. XRP lost 13.8% in November 2025 and 14.8% in December, and in January 2026, it fell another 10.6%.

So far in 2026, XRP has lost value in seven of 11 months. The token's biggest monthly drops this year came in June and November, when it lost 22.1% and 25.4%, respectively, according to CryptoRank. Its best month was August, with a 30% gain.

On Nov. 17, U.S. spot XRP ETFs had $1.08 billion in assets, according to SoSoValue. Since the start of November, the funds have lost about $26 million to outflows, and $19.13 million of that amount left in the week ending Nov. 13. Ripple's next monthly escrow release of 1 billion XRP is scheduled for Dec. 1.

Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse also commented on the sell-off on CNBC on Nov. 19. "What happens is you've got risk-on assets and people are taking liquidity out of a very liquid market, crypto included — Bitcoin, XRP — and moving it into SpaceX. The exact same thing happened coming into the Anthropic IPO. What we saw after the SpaceX IPO was a rally in the crypto markets. I think you'll see that happen here as well," he said.

XRP's November drop took the token to its lowest level in two years, according to TradingView data.

#5066. Ripple’s Voting Stake In XRP Treasury Gets Capped

Home » Ripple (XRP) » Ripple’s Voting Stake In XRP Treasury Gets Capped

Ripple’s voting rights on XRP-only treasury are limited to 9.99%, implying decentralization’s the status quo.

Ripple’s exposure to the XRP-only Evernorth corporate treasury since the NASDAQ debut has been an open debate. Since the October merger, Evernorth’s holdings have imposed a limitation on voting rights ownership, specifically capped at 9.99%.

This gives Ripple Lab’s executives the ability to retain economies of scale through a separate subsidiary class. That implies a strong distinction between financial exposure and voting control. The restriction doesn’t mean Ripple surrendered their additional economic exposure.

Actually, the equity interests exceeding the permitted share allocation remained in the Pathfinder units, which carry distinct rights from ordinary publicly-traded Evernorth(XRPN) shares. The complexity behind Evernorth’s $1 billion corporate treasury fund is laid out in a SEC filing.

SEC FILING: Evernorth files a prospectus supplement to register the resale of up to 73.7M Class A shares and 239,653 sponsor warrants.The company will not receive proceeds from the resale, except on exercise of the sponsor warrants.Last reported price (Nov 16): $7.45 per… https://t.co/wXcQPJQwS5 pic.twitter.com/FakZLekF9s

The company also had cash from its SPAC combination and later financing like convertible notes & practical warrant exercises.

Other Evernorth fund investors received miscellaneous ownership arrangements. SBI-affiliated investors were subject to a 39.9% ownership threshold, while the sponsor’s allocation reflected a 19.9% XRPN voting threshold. Evernorth’s XRPN debuted on NASDAQ on October 12, 2026.

Here’s how the XRP-powered trading fund has been performing on NASDAQ so far:

As previously reported by DailyCoin, Evernorth mulls issuing more than 5.2 million new shares through a financing arrangement with Cantor, which could pull in enough capital for considerable XRP purchases to support the corporate fund’s exponential growth.

At an assumed $10 share price, selling the full allocation would generate approximately $50.6 million before expenses, according to the prospectus. Meanwhile, the Ripple-based exchange traded funds (ETFs) slimmed by $6.14 million over the past 7 days.

With Wall Street’s big boys reducing their XRP exposure based on Spot market’s XRP pricing, it’s clear that crypto whales aren’t keen on buying the dip right now. Judging from the real-time figures on the Chaikin Money Flow (CMF) on multiple time-frames, big-time investors are selling.

With the Parabolic Stop & Reverse (SAR) meter flashing above the current XRP price, this trend signal is a signature of ongoing bearish persistence. Alongside the Chaikin Money Flow (CMF) dwelling in red, the current $0.90 support level could fall into the broader $0.65 liquidity pocket.

#5065. Privacy tokens face a new challenge – Balancing anonymity with oversight

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

On the 1st of October 2025, entrepreneur and angel investor Naval Ravikant posted on X that,

Bitcoin is insurance against fiat. Zcash is insurance against Bitcoin.

Neither of the latter two tokens has managed to regain the highs they made back then.

Meanwhile, ZEC reached $1,700. The altcoin’s correction in recent months back below $1,000 has sparked bearish short-term sentiment, even though it was still trading above the previous year’s peak.

Sophisticated on-chain analysis can still narrow down to the 16 possibilities over multiple “hops”, or intermediate payments or asset transfers along a transaction trail.

Monero’s Full Chain Membership Proofs++ (FCMP++) eliminates the need for decoys. It replaces rings by providing zero-knowledge membership proofs across the entire blockchain output set. In other words, it mathematically proves that the spent XMR belongs to the Monero blockchain, making every previous output created part of the decoy set.

A mainnet activation date has not been confirmed for the FCMP++ and Carrot, the addressing protocol and wallet key hierarchy upgrade designed for the FCMP++ network upgrade. AMBCrypto reported that zkSecurity’s audit of FCMP++ was “complete, zero-knowledge, and sound”.

Using zero-knowledge cryptography, private ZEC balances and transactions are tracked in shielded pools. Zcash has five of these shielded pools, as well as its transparent pool. Once funds are inside, the older pools can not be shut down, but can be made spend-only.

The Ironwood (NU6.3) upgrade was launched on the 28th of July, 2026, addressing the Orchard “infinity” bug found in May. It also introduced a new shielded pool and restricted the Orchard pool. Funds exiting Orchard must now pass through Zcash’s turnstile, for supply integrity verification, to enter Ironwood.

The NU7 upgrade implemented in November aims to reduce block processing times by 67%, from 75 seconds to 25 seconds. It also reduced the wallet scanning workload required to perform private payments.

When transactions are shielded in some form, anti-money laundering (AML) tracing is hindered or prevented. This brings strict regulatory restrictions on shielded transactions, as they require visibility in fund flows to prevent illicit financing.

The latter argues that oversight is a necessity to prevent serious crimes and to detect and disrupt illicit activity.

Selective disclosure, such as in Zcash, Midnight, or using Identity Frameworks in Ethereum, can achieve this by sharing transaction details without revealing the entire financial history.

Zero-knowledge compliance is another approach that can prove that a transaction meets certain criteria, without exposing other relevant details.

#5063. Adam Back Explains Why Bitcoin (BTC) Fell to $17,000 on Kraken's Canadian Dollar Market

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Blockstream CEO Adam Back has explained the overnight sell-off on Kraken's Canadian-dollar markets that sent the exchange's BTC/CAD pair to C$24,250, or roughly $17,000, on Saturday morning, and said that the drop started with the exchange's local USD/CAD market maker.

The Blockstream CEO addressed the issue on X on Saturday in a reply to a post that tracked large sell orders hitting Kraken's BTC/CAD order book at regular intervals throughout the night.

The $17,000 figure is based on the Bank of Canada's Friday rate of C$1.4325 per U.S. dollar. Kraken's BTC/CAD pair printed the C$24,250 low on Saturday morning, according to TradingView data.

"No, the reason was USDCAD Kraken local spot FX market-maker ran out of money, as it has depegged away from market rate 1.434," Back replied.

In the same post, the Blockstream CEO said that it was "more accurate to say you could buy CAD discounted."

Later, another X user argued that the Canadian dollar was in fact overvalued on Kraken during the sell-off, since anyone who held CAD could buy anything at a discount. Back agreed with the correction.

"You are right. BTCUSD normal price, USDCAD CAD mispriced, like a shortage of CAD bid the price up," Back wrote.

On Sunday, the Blockstream CEO described the whole chain of events in two more posts on X.

According to Back, if the USD/CAD market maker failed, there were few buyers of BTC in Canadian dollars, and so the BTC/CAD price fell. In his view, traders would normally close such a gap with an arbitrage trade "by selling USD to CAD to buy if it BTCCAD doesn't track BTCUSD."

Back wrote that the drop "liquidated people" and triggered stop losses "based on a local false price."

"And then USDCAD followed because you could buy BTC cheaply in CAD, so it became logical to buy CAD way over par to take that arb," he added.

The BTC/CAD pair recovered later on Saturday, and on Sunday, it traded at C$86,236 on Kraken, according to TradingView.

Kraken said in a statement that its Canadian-dollar trading pairs faced heavy selling pressure starting around 20:00 UTC on Friday, and prices on these pairs fell significantly below those on other markets.

The Kraken sell-off started on the same day that Bitcoin dropped to $59,370, its lowest level since July 2, according to CoinGlass data. On Friday, CoinGlass also recorded $1.57 billion in liquidations across the crypto market over 24 hours, and long positions accounted for $1.43 billion of that amount.

Still, the exchange said that every order was processed correctly and that its security teams found no evidence of a breach or compromised accounts.

#5062. ‘Price exposure, not the share itself’ — What do tokenized stock holders actually get?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Crypto exchanges spent the past week rolling out tokenized U.S stocks to buyers around the world. On an unrelated note, Nvidia slid by 30% from its ATH in a month following Jensen Huang’s departure. All while AMD rose — an early sign of decoupling.

One story is about tokenization, while the other is about AI stocks. However, both skirt around the same topic: a growing class of investors being handed exposure they don’t fully understand by markets that do not care to explain it.

For most people outside the U.S, buying an Apple or Nvidia stock is hard. It is either expensive, tedious, or simply unavailable. Pritam Dutta, Founder and CEO of Zoth, explained the same when he spoke to AMBCrypto, stating,

There’s a lot of excitement around tokenized stocks right now. Most people outside the U.S. still can’t easily buy shares in Apple, Nvidia and the rest of the Magnificent Seven. Tokenization changes that.

Undoubtedly, for a buyer in Nigeria or the Philippines, a tokenized stock could be their first realistic route into U.S markets. But Dutta’s next point is the one that should make an investor sit up and take notice.

A lot of what’s launching gives you price exposure, not the share itself. You don’t get voting rights, and dividends depend on the platform’s terms… Too few platforms are being upfront about it.

In a nutshell, investors are not buying Apple. They are buying a token that tracks Apple’s price, issued by a platform whose terms determine what else you get, subject to its discretion.

Dutta, then, reduced the entire conversation to three questions investors must ask themselves before running towards such stocks.

Who holds the real share? What happens to my dividends? What happens if the platform fails?

These are not clever questions. These are the questions you should ask before buying anything that is marketed as “tokenized.” Unfortunately, the fact that they need to be asked in the first place is a sure-shot sign of how little the platforms are disclosing.

In today’s age, the access is real, but the understanding might not be. And, that gap is the hardest on buyers with the least room to make an error.

Nvidia fell this week while AMD rose. The easy explanation is Jensen Huang’s exit. However, Ian Weisberger, CEO of CoinRoutes, believes that there is a larger cause. He told AMBCrypto,

The real problem is that Nvidia’s chips are becoming a commodity.

For years, Nvidia and the AI boom were not seen as two separate entities, simply because of the former’s chips. That is also how the company grew to roughly 8% of the S&P 500.

This week, though the market began pulling the two apart — and within the AI sector — the separation was underway long before anyone realized.

Anyone who has built on both stacks knows the gap is closing. As the chips become interchangeable, buyers will shop on price, and the profits will move to the companies building products on top of them.

That is the concentration problem in one paragraph. When a single stock is 8% of the AI sector, millions of retail investors own a large position in it through their index funds. That is exposure they never signed up to be a part of.

The AI boom is not ending, but the era of one stock carrying the index might be.

Both stories have the same problem — the people most likely to be hurt are unfortunately the least equipped to protect themselves. Here’s why.

Firstly, tokenized stocks are best for an overseas buyer for whom a U.S brokerage account is out of reach. Unfortunately, that buyer is also the first to suffer losses if their platform of choice fails.

Secondly, when it comes to artificial intelligence, the cracks are less noticeable. Most overseas retail investors own Nvidia through an index fund that was marketed to them as “diversified.” This causes the investor with the least information to hold one of the most important market bets.

As Nicolai Sondergaard, research analyst at Nansen, told AMBCrypto:

AI and crypto draw on the same pool of risk capital, so when Nvidia sells off, the money goes to cash, not into Bitcoin.

At the time of Sondergaard’s analysis, Nvidia was down about 32% from its 6th October high, Bitcoin [BTC] was 26% below its September peak, and gold was 17% off its August high. Meanwhile, the dollar was at a six-month high, and real yields were up more than 0.2 percentage points in two months.

So, the investor who thought they were spread across AI and crypto was actually holding double the risk. And in the worst case, they must have not figured it out before it was too late.

The same story can be crafted across different markets — the sophisticated investor can take on the risk, while the overseas investor cannot. Regardless, the product holds no difference when marketing to them.

The demand is real. The question is whether people are being sold what they think they’re buying.

The question at the end of it all is — were buyers sold a dream that was impossible to achieve? Looking further, was the promised portfolio diversification ever there at all?

In the end, the retail investor’s blind spot is not ignorance. It is the industry that keeps expanding faster than investors understand what’s going on, causing them to buy the narrative and allure. Not the reality.

Cryptocurrencies are of particular interest to Saman, with much of her writing centered around understanding how ideas like Momentum and Greater Fool theories apply to altcoins, specifically, memecoins.

#5061. Three XRP Features Race for Last-Minute 2026 Activation: What Remains

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

In the final stretch of 2026, some XRP Ledger protocol features await mainnet activation, with validator support and amendment voting as the final steps to bring new functionality to the XRPL network.

In light of this, three amendments are racing against the 2026 calendar, with only a few votes left to reach a majority and enter the two-week activation period. These include SingleAssetVault, LendingProtocol and confidential transfers.

All three remain below the required validator approval threshold of over 80% support, making the final weeks of the year increasingly important for their activation prospects.

With about five weeks left in 2026, activation remains possible this year, but additional validator support for each amendment must arrive soon enough and be sustained to complete the required two-week activation window.

The lending protocol allows on-chain fixed-term, uncollateralized loans from pooled funds in a Single Asset Vault. There are two amendments: LendingProtocolV1_1 and the original Lending Protocol amendment.

The focus is on LendingProtocolV1_1, which extends the Lending Protocol and SingleAssetVault amendments with a new closed-ended vault and cash-basis accounting. With the amendment, new loan brokers can only be created against closed-ended vaults.

LendingProtocolV1_1 has reached 70.59% support with 24 out of 34 validators voting yes, according to XRPScan data. It needs 28 votes to reach a majority, or more than 80% of 34 votes, meaning it has just four votes left.

SingleAssetVault creates a structure for aggregating assets from multiple depositors and is intended to be used with the on-chain Lending Protocol. It has gained 73.53% support so far with 25 out of 34 validators voting in favor. Reaching more than 80% of 34 votes requires 28 votes; thus, three votes remain for it to enter the activation window.

#5059. Morning Minute: Google’s Newest AI Model Tops Benchmarks Ahead of Anthropic IPO

Morning Minute: Google’s Newest AI Model Tops Benchmarks Ahead of Anthropic IPO

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

Google shipped Gemini 4 Neon on Wednesday, its fourth frontier model release in three weeks, and on the eve of the heavily anticipated Anthropic IPO.

This isn’t just a normal model release. For one, it’s incredibly good. Neon posts 61 on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index against 59 for Claude Opus 5.5 and 57 for GPT-6.2 Sol. It takes the Vals Index at 70.9%, AutomationBench at 56.3% against Opus 5.5’s 42.5%, and GraphWalks long-context retrieval at 95.2% against Opus 5.5’s 66.8%.

Beyond just the outperformance, Google has been releasing at a pace nobody else is matching. It dropped Argon in mid-October. Carbon on October 28. Hydrogen last week. Google owns its silicon, its data centers, and its distribution, and it is using all three to iterate faster than labs that rent compute. And it has some speculating that Google has already achieved RSI (recursive self-improvement), the final step ahead of super intelligence.

So Google has caught up at the top of the AI race. Just in time for the ANT IPO.

Anthropic will be priced at $83, 6.4% above the top of its $70 to $78 range, valuing the company around $1.5 trillion fully diluted. On the 825 million shares in the original terms, that’s roughly $68.5 billion raised, and about $94.5 billion counting the separate $26 billion private placement from its compute suppliers. It trades Thursday on Nasdaq as ANT. Notably, ANT has been trading on Hyperliquid in the $90-$100 range for the past week but popped to $105 this morning.

The valuation math is a 55% step-up from the $965 billion May round and about 25% below the $2 trillion Anthropic was reportedly chasing in September. Revenue ran $33.2 billion through nine months against $2.5 billion a year earlier, a 13x increase. Annualize that and $1.5 trillion is roughly 34 times sales.

That’s a steep raise given the macro backdrop and the Fed calling for more hikes for longer. AI stocks have been shaky over the past month and even moreso since Jensen Huang left Nvidia. Not the most confident market headed into the 2nd $1T+ IPO of the year.

So what does it mean for today’s price action? Personally, I expect a pop to the $95-$105 level that Hyperliquid has been pricing out for weeks, and it likely sees a spike higher. But there will be massive volatility, and it’s very likely to trade under that $83 level over the coming days and weeks. Especially given the new (surprise) headwinds from Google with its outperforming model.

We will find out soon enough. Good luck to everyone trading it today.

#5058. Ripple USD (RLUSD) Breaks Into Global Top Five Stablecoins as Supply Hits New ATH

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. Ripple’s dollar-pegged stablecoin, Ripple USD (RLUSD), has now entered the top five stablecoins by market cap, reaching a new all-time high in its circulating supply, a major milestone for the stablecoin less than two years after its launch. RLUSD went live in December 2024.

RLUSD has surpassed World Liberty Financial USD (USD1), Global Dollar (USDG) and PayPal USD (PYUSD) to become the fifth-largest stablecoin in the world, according to data from CoinMarketCap. Only Tether’s USDT, Circle’s USDC, Ethena’s USDe, and Dai (DAI) are ahead of it.

The milestone is proof of the rapid growth RLUSD has experienced in a competitive stablecoin market dominated by established players like Tether’s USDT and Circle’s USDC.

RLUSD’s total circulating supply across the XRP Ledger and Ethereum has reached a new high, with $2.85 billion in circulation, according to Ripple’s stablecoin tracker.

Ethereum commands $2.089 billion of this total figure, while $768 million in supply sits on the XRP Ledger. While Ethereum currently holds the larger share of RLUSD’s supply, XRPL has seen significant activity in RLUSD settlement volumes.

In March 2025, during a Bloomberg TV interview, Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse projected that RLUSD would enter the top five stablecoins; that projection has now been fulfilled.

According to RWA Foundation, Ripple led weekly stablecoin growth, adding $75.4 million in the last seven days. This growth was reflected in RLUSD's market cap, allowing it to surpass World Liberty Financial USD to clinch the fifth spot in global stablecoin rankings.

Former Ripple executive Anthony Welfare highlighted this growth in a recent X post, saying, “RLUSD is the only major stablecoin growing right now.”

Welfare noted that RLUSD supply is up nearly 20% in three weeks, rising from $2.42 billion to $2.9 billion, while USDT has fallen by $5 billion and USDC by $3 billion over the same period.

The XRP Ledger accounts for the majority of RLUSD settlements: in an analogy on X, Welfare noted that XRPL holds about 27% of RLUSD’s supply but processed 57% of its volume last week, amounting to $6.7 billion versus Ethereum, which recorded $5 billion.

#5057. XRP Ledger trial settled 213,000 paid AI requests in 6,324 transactions

t54 Labs reported an average network fee of 0.36 drops per paid request in its closed October beta. Two November checks paid the same merchant in RLUSD, with individual requests counted off-ledger.

Two checks cashed on the XRP Ledger on Nov. 18 paid the same AI service in RLUSD. Payments company t54 Labs identified both as batches. In its closed October beta, t54 reported that bundling requests cut the average network fee to 0.36 drops of XRP per paid call.

The checks delivered 0.31 RLUSD and 0.46 RLUSD to the same ledger address. The XRPL AI Hub, run by t54 Labs, identifies the merchant as Heurist Inference Router and classifies both transfers as batches.

The first check and second check came from different payer addresses. Each had a spending ceiling of 1 RLUSD. The ledger charged 12 drops of XRP to open each check and 12 to cash it, for a total of 24 drops per pair.

A drop is one millionth of an XRP. The Hub marks payment intent as unverified in its first receipt and its second receipt. The receipts give no call counts, so the November fee per request remains unknown.

t54 Labs reported that its mainnet beta settled 213,008 paid requests across 3,162 sessions using 6,324 ledger transactions from Oct. 14 to 17. It put the average network fee at about 0.36 drops per paid request. Its benchmark was a single transaction per request at the ledger's standard minimum fee of 10 drops, which can rise under load.

In t54's beta, the payer first opens a check on the ledger with a maximum amount. A session key authorizes each charge off-ledger as the agent makes paid requests. At the end of the session, the merchant cashes the check once for the amount used. Each completed session takes two ledger transactions, so adding requests to a session spreads those two fees over more charges.

The beta began on mainnet in October with RLUSD as its only settlement asset. t54 says it is limited to onboarded merchants and registered payers, and its developer guide still describes sessions as part of internal merchant testing. The public facilitator has yet to offer batch sessions, according to t54.

An open check adds 0.2 XRP to the payer's reserve requirement on top of the regular 1 XRP account reserve. That amount remains in the account while the check is open. A payer with several checks open at once needs the extra reserve for each one. The reserve requirement falls when the check is cashed or canceled. Expiration alone leaves the check on the ledger until someone cancels it.

#5056. Rethinking crypto risk — Here’s why ONE factor doesn’t tell the whole story

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Investors have a habit of gauging crypto by a single risk framework. However, currently, that framework no longer captures how the market works.

Bitcoin’s [BTC] daily price fluctuations are about three times wider than the S&P 500’s, with far more extreme moves in both directions. However, the realized volatility of Bitcoin has declined over time to the level of individual large-cap U.S stocks.

So, investors should be aware of three things — where returns come from, how they fit inside a portfolio, and how crypto has grown far beyond Bitcoin.

The standard risk tools like the standard deviation or the Sharpe ratio don’t care how much money you make or lose. That leaves a blind spot for Bitcoin as most of its long-term gains come from just a handful of trading days.

Bitcoin has averaged 60% per year over the last three years. If you take away its ten best days, that number drops to 7%. If you take away its ten worst days, the number jumps to 197%. That’s because big drops are often followed by big rebounds, so investors who try to avoid bad days can easily miss the best ones.

Momentum counts as well. Bitcoin’s gains tend to pile on top of each other, and Bitcoin runs far better after months of winning than months of losing. During strong rallies, the Sortino ratio can rise well above normal risk scores. Mostly because it only measures returns against downside volatility, not upside volatility.

Notably, Bitcoin has dropped by 50% or more seven times since 2011. But when you include the volatility of starting, Bitcoin’s falls look more like stocks and bonds.

In fact, even “low risk” bonds have been falling for a while in recent years.

While big price moves can be alarming on their own, portfolio managers care most about how an asset behaves when put together with the other assets they own. Changes in stocks, bonds, and the private market are mostly driven by common economic factors. However, these factors explain the changes in Bitcoin much less.

Cryptocurrencies, including Bitcoin, have historically shown low correlation with traditional measures than with each other. This is the case, even though correlations can increase during periods of market stress.

Large organizations have taken this into account too. For instance, J.P. Morgan Asset Management, an organization with $5 trillion in assets, introduced Bitcoin into its 2027 outlook, estimating a 10% per year return in the coming ten years. This correlates by only 33% with major U.S. equities, even less than international equities, corporate bonds, and hedge funds, even when there is 55% volatility.

This provides a unique combination of both high expected returns and little correlation with core equities. History proves that including a small portion of Bitcoin in an average institutional portfolio can increase returns and efficiency without increasing overall risk or maximum losses.

Notably, Mike Belshe, CEO of BitGo, recently pointed out JPMorgan’s changing perspective on crypto. He said,

Let that sink in. The world’s largest bank, long crypto-skeptical, is now publicly supporting crypto market structure legislation. This is a different ballgame.

Another mistake is treating crypto as a single Bitcoin bet. The modern landscape of crypto includes smart contract platforms, decentralized finance, consumer applications, and artificial intelligence. Each having its own unique users and economics.

Over the last three years, Bitcoin has accounted for just about 50% of volatility in returns on all major crypto sectors, while correlations among sectors have decreased.

Out of the top 10 biggest non-stablecoin crypto assets by market cap, six different tokens have been leading returns on a quarter-by-quarter basis over the last two years. Bitcoin has never led once.

None of this implies that crypto is risk-free though. Especially since a rally could unwind quickly. On the contrary, risk is not something that can be boiled down to one simple figure.

A uniform know-how about crypto implies understanding of multiple factors — volatility of the asset, distribution of returns, correlation with the rest of a portfolio, and the performance of various sectors separately.

Therefore, in reality, instead of trying to overlook any price fluctuations, it is important to view them with a proper perspective.

Rather than discussing the volatility of crypto per se, one should think of what kind of risks it introduces, what risks from one’s portfolio it offsets, and what additional sources of profit appear outside of Bitcoin.

#5055. 5 Best VPNs Worth Using for Crypto

Home » Cryptopedia » 5 Best VPNs Worth Using for Crypto

As global tracking and exchange geofencing tighten, choosing the right VPN matters more than ever.

If you’re involved in crypto, you know how quickly things can go wrong: one wrong click, an unsecured public Wi-Fi connection or a data leak can expose your wallet, trading activity or identity.

In 2026 the stakes are higher. Crypto adoption is growing, DeFi is expanding, AI capabilities are advancing, and more jurisdictions are looking to monitor financial activity.

Your internet service provider (ISP), exchange or local network may be able to see your IP address and traffic patterns. Public Wi-Fi remains a common security risk, while geo-restrictions on exchanges are still widespread. If your IP address is linked to a wallet or trading account, the risk of further identification increases.

A good VPN encrypts your connection, hides your real IP address and reduces this exposure. It does not replace hardware wallets, two-factor authentication (2FA) or sound operational security. It is just one layer of protection.

To identify the strongest options for 2026, we evaluated providers against these key criteria:

Instead, Mullvad generates a random 16-digit account number. You can add funds using Bitcoin (BTC), Bitcoin Cash (BCH), Monero (XMR) or even physical cash sent by mail. The price is a flat €5 per month, with no long-term discounts or renewal price increases. Cryptocurrency payments qualify for a 10% discount.

Mullvad runs its own full node for each supported cryptocurrency and hosts all its wallets, so no third party is involved.

Mullvad has undergone multiple independent security audits. In a 2023 police raid, authorities reportedly found no customer data to seize. The service uses RAM-only servers, open-source apps and the WireGuard protocol. It supports up to five devices.

Best for: Users who want to provide as little personal information as possible when signing up.

Proton VPN has one of the most consistent public audit records among the providers reviewed. The Swiss-based company passed its fifth consecutive annual no-logs audit by Securitum in June 2026.

Payment options are more limited. Bitcoin is available only after you create a free account and upgrade to a paid plan. Proton also accepts cash in CHF, USD and EUR.

Proton VPN operates more than 20,000 servers in over 130 countries. Its apps are open source, and paid plans support up to 10 devices.

Unlike providers that use RAM-only servers, Proton VPN relies on full-disk encryption. For leak protection, it offers a standard kill switch and a permanent kill switch on Windows and Linux, which blocks internet access if the VPN connection drops.

Best for: Users who value audited transparency and Swiss jurisdiction and are comfortable providing an email address.

NordVPN stands out for its performance and range of security features. Based in Panama, where no data-retention laws apply, it uses RAM-only servers. Its no-logs policy has been independently verified six times, most recently by Deloitte Lithuania in late 2025.

Features include an internet kill switch, obfuscated servers, Dedicated IP options and Threat Protection, which blocks malicious websites and phishing links. NordVPN supports up to 10 simultaneous connections.

Payment options include Bitcoin, Ethereum, Litecoin and stablecoins.

Best for: Active traders who prioritise speed, Dedicated IP options and a broad set of features.

Surfshark combines competitive pricing with a growing record of independent audits. Its Trust Center lists Deloitte audits from 2023 and 2025 that verified its no-logs policy. KPMG audit also confirmed Surfshark does not store browsing history, IP addresses, connection timestamps or outgoing traffic.

Crypto users can pay with Bitcoin, Ethereum, Cardano, Dogecoin, XRP and Shiba Inu through supported third-party wallets. However, an email address is required to register.

Surfshark is based in the Netherlands. The company says local law does not require it to log or retain user activity. Its entire server network uses RAM-only infrastructure.

Security features include an automatic kill switch and Everlink, which switches users to another server during maintenance without interrupting their session.

Signup generates a random account ID and requires no email address or other personal information. Payment options include Monero, Bitcoin, physical cash, credit cards and PayPal. The company also runs its own nodes for cryptocurrency payments.

IVPN’s apps are fully open source. Built-in features include AntiTracker, a kill switch/firewall and V2Ray obfuscation.

The company has commissioned annual security audits by Cure53. These reviews assess its apps and infrastructure; they are not equivalent to a Big Four audit specifically designed to verify no-logs claims.

In August 2026, a vulnerability in BTCPay Server allowed an attacker to drain 0.6168 BTC from IVPN’s Lightning node. IVPN says no customer data was accessed.

A VPN hides your IP address, protects traffic on public Wi-Fi and limits what your internet provider can see. But it does not make your crypto wallet anonymous.

It can if you use a shared IP address that jumps across countries or lands on an exchange’s blacklist. Centralized exchanges (CEXs) frequently flag sudden IP changes as suspicious login attempts or sanctions-evasion risks. If you trade regularly on a CEX, stick to a single server location or use a Dedicated IP add-on so your login address stays consistent.

Almost never. Most free VPNs monetize users by logging traffic, injecting ads, or selling connection metadata to third parties—which defeats the purpose of crypto operational security. The only exception on this list is Proton VPN’s free tier, which has no data caps, no ads, and is covered by the same independent no-logs audits as its paid plans, though it limits server locations and doesn’t support direct crypto payments without upgrading.

Yes. HTTPS encrypts the contents of your connection, but it does not hide your IP address or the domains you connect to. Without a VPN, your internet provider can still see that you are connecting to crypto exchanges or DeFi front-ends, and your wallet’s default RPC node can still log your home IP alongside your public wallet address.

#5054. Bitcoin's (BTC) Worst Is Behind After $55,000 Low, 4-Year Cycle Theory Suggests

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Bitcoin (BTC) fell as low as $55,081 on Nov. 21, its lowest price since September 2024, according to TradingView data. The cryptocurrency then bounced back and was trading at $60,583 on Sunday, after it opened November at $77,425.

Bitcoin has lost 21.6% so far in November 2026, according to CryptoRank. The last time Bitcoin had a worse month was June 2022, when the cryptocurrency lost 37.9%, according to the same data.

The four-year cycle theory ties Bitcoin's market cycles to halvings, and its supporters point to 2014, 2018 and 2022 as the years when each bear market went through its final sell-off. In this context, 2026 is the next year in that row, and the next Bitcoin halving is expected in April 2028. The previous three halvings took place in July 2016, May 2020 and April 2024.

Bitcoin set its record above $1,100 in late 2013. In February 2014, the Japanese exchange Mt. Gox, which handled most Bitcoin trading in 2013, filed for bankruptcy after it lost about 850,000 BTC. In November 2014, Bitcoin gained 11.6%, according to CryptoRank, but December of that year brought a 15.3% drop, and the bottom below $200 came in January 2015.

In December 2017, Bitcoin reached a record near $20,000 on the back of the ICO boom, and during 2018, the ICO market dried up. In November 2018, the crypto market crashed after the Bitcoin Cash hard fork and the so-called hash war between the two camps of miners, and Bitcoin lost 36.4% that month. The cryptocurrency then lost another 6.4% in December 2018 and set its cycle low at about $3,200 in the middle of that month.

The 2021 record near $69,000 came in November 2021. In May 2022, the Terra ecosystem collapsed, and in July of that year, Celsius and Three Arrows Capital filed for bankruptcy. In November of that year, the FTX exchange went bankrupt. Bitcoin lost 16.1% in November 2022 and set its bear market bottom at around $15,500 on Nov. 21, 2022, four years to the day before the latest low. December of that year brought a 3.7% loss, and in January 2023, Bitcoin gained 39.9%.

In the current cycle, Bitcoin set its record above $126,000 in October 2025. In 2026, Bitcoin has lost value in six of the 11 months so far, according to CryptoRank. On Nov. 20, the cryptocurrency fell below $60,000 for the first time since July, and the decline continued the next day.

The latest drop comes amid a broad sell-off on Wall Street, where technology and AI stocks came under pressure, and amid expectations of further interest-rate hikes by the Federal Reserve.
