#4951. Kalshi faces Monday hearing on New York order it says would shut down its exchange nationwide

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

A Manhattan state judge will hear New York's request on Monday for an order that Kalshi says would close its exchange "for every customer, everywhere," though New York's AG said that's not her goal.

A federal judge in Manhattan on Friday rejected the Commodity Futures Trading Commission's second emergency bid to stop New York Attorney General Letitia James from pursuing her case against Kalshi, a win for the state that clears the way for a state court to decide Monday morning on an order that Kalshi says would shut down its exchange nationwide.

U.S. District Judge Lorna Schofield denied the request from the bench after a telephone hearing that began at 5 p.m. ET Friday. In a letter to the state court that evening, James' office said Schofield found the CFTC "was unable to show a likelihood of success on the merits that the CEA [Commodity Exchange Act] preempted state gaming laws."

The CFTC has been arguing in lawsuits against several states that the CEA means prediction markets like Kalshi and Polymarket are federally regulated, and thus state-level restrictions on sports betting activity don't apply to them. U.S. District Judge Jed Rakoff had turned down the agency's first emergency request to stop enforcement in New York on July 31, the day James filed suit.

Justice Melissa Crane of the New York County Supreme Court will hear James' request for a temporary restraining order at 11:30 a.m. ET on Monday, according to sports and gaming attorney Daniel Wallach. The case returned to Crane on Thursday after U.S. District Judge Vernon Broderick rejected Kalshi's arguments for keeping it in federal court.

James' proposed order would bar Kalshi from operating an unlicensed gambling business "within or from New York or to persons in New York," covering contracts on sports, culture, elections and "other events." It would also bar Kalshi from letting users under 21 trade and from advertising on college campuses.

Kalshi lawyer Sean Murphy of Milbank wrote to Crane on Thursday night that because Kalshi is based in New York, the order "would shut down a federally regulated exchange for every customer, everywhere" and "would likely force the liquidation of all open positions." He added that it would conflict with an Aug. 11 CFTC emergency order directing Kalshi to keep operating.

James' office replied Friday that it "does not seek to 'shut down a federally regulated exchange for every customer, everywhere,'" and that the order would not stop Kalshi from offering lawful futures or swaps. The letter urged Crane to give the CFTC order no weight, citing a Nov. 10 ruling by U.S. District Judge Colleen McMahon that said the order "smacks of a collusive effort between a regulator and regulatee to generate a claim for conflict preemption."

"If Justice Crane adopts the 'from New York' language—effectively imposing a nationwide injunction—the next stop could be the U.S. Supreme Court's emergency docket," Wallach wrote on X on Saturday.The wider states-versus-CFTC fight could end up before the Supreme Court, as federal appeals courts have split on whether states can regulate Kalshi, with the Fourth, Sixth and Ninth Circuits siding with states and the Third Circuit siding with Kalshi.

The Fourth Circuit ruled against Kalshi in its fight with Maryland regulators this week, though the two judges in the majority disagreed on why. Judge Roger Gregory wrote that federal law does not preempt Maryland's gambling laws even if Kalshi's contracts are swaps, while Judge Stephanie Thacker wrote that the contracts are not swaps at all: "What is happening here is sports betting."

The CFTC and the Department of Justice sued New York in April over its efforts to restrict prediction markets in the state, arguing the agency has exclusive authority over event contracts listed on federally registered exchanges, as it has in many other state-level cases. The CFTC's request for a preliminary injunction in that case has been pending since May.

In its Friday filing, the CFTC called James' renewed request "another attempt to fully shutter a CFTC DCM's [designated contract market] operations" and said "New York wants to put Kalshi out of business altogether." The agency said it asked New York that morning to hold off on enforcement until Schofield rules on the injunction, and the state, notably, declined.

Separately, Kalshi CEO Tarek Mansour attended the Democratic Attorneys General Association's meeting in Chicago on Thursday, Politico's Daniel Lippman reported, citing an eyewitness. Kalshi spokesperson Elisabeth Diana confirmed his attendance to Politico but declined to say which attorneys general he met with. Politico did not report whether AG James attended.

The Block has reached out to Kalshi, the CFTC and James' office for comment.

#4950. Hyperliquid Anthropic futures priced within 1% of Nasdaq opening price

Hyperliquid traders priced Anthropic futures within 1% of the stock’s Nasdaq opening price, as trading surged ahead of the AI company’s IPO.

Anthropic perpetual futures on Hyperliquid were priced within 1% of the company’s Nasdaq opening share price Thursday, shortly before the stock began trading.

Anthropic shares opened at $111.50, up 34.3% from the company’s $83 initial public offering (IPO) price, according to several data dashboards. The opening price valued the Claude AI chatbot maker at around $2 trillion.

Just before trading began, Anthropic futures on Hyperliquid’s trade.xyz market were priced at $110.48, according to Senpi at around 4:44 pm UTC. That was just $1.02, or 0.91%, below the stock’s opening price.

The massive debut once again puts crypto’s growing pre-IPO futures market in the spotlight, especially as exchanges compete to attract traders who want to bet on major companies before their shares begin trading on Wall Street. SpaceX’s debut earlier this year also drew interest from crypto traders betting on its stock price through crypto futures.

Perpetual futures allow traders to bet on whether prices will rise or fall without owning the underlying shares, and unlike traditional futures they have no expiration date and can trade around the clock — including before a company goes public.

Trading on Hyperliquid was especially strong just before Anthropic shares opened, with the contract on trade.xyz recording $545.7 million in trading volume over 24 hours and $303.3 million in open interest (OI), according to Senpi data.

The contract was also trading at a 2.58% premium to its oracle price, the same data showed.

This interest had been growing for weeks, according to a Thursday report from crypto research firm Block Scholes, which found that daily trading volume for Anthropic futures on Hyperliquid averaged $75 million since Nov. 5, up from $7 million in September.

Traders also appeared to be betting on Anthropic’s stock rising, according to Token Terminal data, which showed that 5,567 traders held long positions in Anthropic futures on Hyperliquid on Wednesday, compared with 1,996 holding short positions.

Open interest in Anthropic perpetual futures on Hyperliquid reached a record $186 million on Nov. 18. Source: Token Terminal

OI across Anthropic futures markets on Hyperliquid also reached a record $186 million on Wednesday. Of that, $86 million came from trade.xyz contracts, while Entropy.io accounted for about $100 million.

Still, Anthropic futures made up just 1% of Hyperliquid’s total trading volume and accounted for only 3% of the increase in non-crypto trading since September, the Block Scholes report found.

Anthropic shares were trading at $98.49 as of 5:43 pm UTC on Thursday, down about 12% from their $111.50 opening price but still nearly 19% above the $83 IPO price.

#4947. Chainflip’s losses climb to $12 million after protocol discloses 122.9 BTC theft, offers hacker bounty

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Chainflip offered the attacker a 10% bounty to return the rest of the stolen funds by Monday and said they think law enforcement will be able to track them down based on metadata they left behind.

Cross-chain decentralized exchange Chainflip Chainflip (FLIP) said an attacker took 122.86 BTC from its Bitcoin vault on Saturday, on top of the $4.86 million in ETH and stablecoins drained from its Ethereum vault that The Block reported Saturday, putting total losses at about $12 million.

Chainflip Labs, in a message to the hacker, said 122.86137042 BTC, 520.22 ETH, 3.25 million USDC and 758,141 USDT were taken from its vaults. The bitcoin was worth about $7.1 million at the time of the theft.

In its message, which was also sent onchain, Chainflip Labs offered to let the hacker keep 10% of the proceeds and stop its law enforcement efforts if they return the remaining 90% by Monday at 1:38 p.m. ET. Chainflip also says it has identified and fixed the vulnerability that the attacker exploited, and that it has learned some details about the attacker, including identifying the server that the hacker used to register their validator account.

"You have made some mistakes in how you conducted this attack and we believe law enforcement will be able to establish an identity based on the metadata you left behind and the particular services you used," the message states. "Your addresses are flagged with the major blockchain analytics firms, which will make it extremely difficult to liquidate the proceeds of your actions, and every withdrawal from the Tornado Cash pools you used is being watched. We will pursue this as hard as we can, for as long as it takes."

Chainflip did not describe the precise mechanism the attacker exploited, though it appears to have been a compromise of the protocol's aggregate signing key. Chainflip's vaults are controlled by signing keys held jointly by a set of up to 150 validators, 100 of whom must take part to approve a transaction.

The hacker stole the bitcoin from Chainflip's vault about 30 minutes before the Ethereum drain that security firms flagged on Saturday. At 1:41 a.m. ET, a single transaction moved 122.86 BTC from Chainflip's Bitcoin vault to a new address, and the funds moved again about 20 minutes later, onchain data shows.

The Block previously reported that Chainflip's Bitcoin vault appeared to be unaffected. A separate transfer that moved about 10.5 BTC from a previous Chainflip vault address to a new address at 6:24 a.m. ET, which The Block also reported, is not among the assets Chainflip listed as stolen, suggesting that movement was Chainflip preventing further losses.

The 122.86 BTC remained at the attacker's second address as of Sunday afternoon. The ETH and stablecoins taken from the Ethereum vault were swapped and sent through Tornado Cash within ten minutes in a process that appeared to be fully automated, according to blockchain security firm AMLBot.

Chainflip founder Simon Harman said on X on Sunday morning that he has been in contact with law enforcement and investigators and is "now relatively confident we can get back and running despite the significant losses to LPs." Harman said Chainflip will publish a report on the incident on Monday and that ideas for compensation are still being worked on.

Chainflip's website says its services are temporarily unavailable. FLIP traded at about $0.30 on Sunday afternoon, down about 37% from its level before the attack, according to The Block's Chainflip price page.

In September, Chainflip lost about 736,000 USDT when an attacker abused a flaw in how the protocol processed Tron transaction memos. Its validators later voted to reimburse affected liquidity providers using surplus funds held in the protocol's vaults.

#4946. MoonPay seeks US bank charter for stablecoin, custody services

MoonPay’s proposed national trust bank would offer stablecoin issuance and redemption, reserve management and digital asset custody under federal oversight.

Crypto payments firm MoonPay has applied for a US national trust bank charter to offer stablecoin issuance and redemption, reserve management and digital asset custody services.

According to an OCC filing, MoonPay submitted its application on Tuesday to establish MoonPay National Trust Bank, a non-depository institution headquartered in Miami Beach, Florida. The proposed bank would not accept traditional deposits or issue loans.

MoonPay already provides stablecoin issuance infrastructure to businesses, including support for branded digital dollars. If approved, the charter would allow its proposed banking subsidiary to provide stablecoin and custody services under federal supervision.

The application comes as US regulators prepare to implement the GENIUS Act, which takes effect on Jan. 18, 2027, and establishes federal standards for stablecoin issuers, including reserve backing and redemption requirements.

With the filing, MoonPay joins several crypto and payments companies seeking federal trust bank charters, including Rain, Modern Treasury and Block. Coinbase’s charter took effect earlier this month, while Circle received final OCC approval in July.

The rise in crypto firms applying for federal trust bank charters has drawn opposition from community banks. In October, the Independent Community Bankers of America sued the OCC, arguing that the regulator exceeded its authority by allowing non-depository trust banks to conduct activities traditionally associated with commercial banks.

#4945. MoonPay Applies for National Trust Bank Charter to Grow Custody and Stablecoin Business

MoonPay Applies for National Trust Bank Charter to Grow Custody and Stablecoin Business

Crypto payments firm MoonPay has applied for a U.S. national bank charter, joining a growing list of crypto companies seeking a federal banking license.

The proposed MoonPay National Trust Bank would be based in Miami Beach, Florida, according to the company's November 17 application to the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC), Bloomberg reported on Thursday. The charter would help MoonPay grow its custody business and expand its stablecoin services, according to the application.

MoonPay confirmed the move on Thursday, tweeting that it was "proud to share" that it had applied for a U.S. national bank charter with the OCC.

today we are proud to share that MoonPay has applied for a US National Bank Charter with the OCC 🇺🇸 pic.twitter.com/XGHBPx84v0

Unlike a full-service bank, a national trust bank can hold and manage assets for clients, but can't take deposits or lend money. The OCC did not immediately respond to Bloomberg's request for comment.

Founded in 2019, MoonPay is best known for providing on- and off-ramps between fiat currencies and crypto. It has been expanding into new areas including AI, having launched a crypto wallet designed for AI chatbots, let users buy crypto through ChatGPT, and acquired AI trading copilot startup Dawn Labs.

It has also been courting institutions. In April, it acquired Sodot, a Tel Aviv-based crypto key management firm, to form the basis of MoonPay Institutional, a unit serving banks, asset managers and trading firms. The unit is led by former acting Commodity Futures Trading Commission chair Caroline Pham.

MoonPay is the latest crypto firm to seek a federal charter under President Donald Trump's administration. Earlier this month, Coinbase joined Circle and Ripple in receiving final OCC approval for a national trust bank, after Ripple won approval for a trust bank behind its RLUSD stablecoin. Jack Dorsey's Block has also applied for a charter to custody Bitcoin, while World Liberty Financial, the crypto venture tied to the Trump family, won conditional approval for its own trust company in August.

The approvals have drawn pushback. A banking group has sued over Block's application, accusing crypto firms of seeking "side door" access to banking, while Sen. Elizabeth Warren (D-MA) has argued that the OCC's approvals of Coinbase and Ripple were illegal, a claim the crypto industry has disputed.

Warren is expected to chair the Senate Banking Committee from January, and is expected to examine World Liberty's charter as part of a wider look at Trump-linked crypto ventures.

#4943. Trump to host second memecoin dinner tonight with TRUMP down 99% from peak

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

One holder nabbed a seat with only $518 worth of TRUMP, a huge drop from the roughly $55,000 worth that holders required for the first dinner in May 2025, according to The Washington Post.

President Donald Trump is set to host a dinner on Sunday night for the largest holders of his Official Trump Official Trump (TRUMP) memecoin at Trump National Golf Club in Northern Virginia, outside Washington, according to the event's website. It is the second dinner and the third event overall for top TRUMP holders since the token launched in January 2025.

Fight Fight Fight LLC, the firm that promotes the memecoin, invited the top 185 holders based on their average TRUMP holdings between Sept. 30 and Nov. 16. One holder reached No. 179 after buying just $518 worth of TRUMP in October and November, according to a Washington Post analysis published Wednesday. It took at least $55,000 worth of the token to qualify for the first dinner, the Post found.

The Post's analysis also found more than 5,000 holders who could have qualified by registering but did not, even after organizers extended sign-ups by four days.

The top 29 holders are invited to a VIP reception with Trump and one of the event's celebrity guests, and the top 120 will receive gift bags that include a Trump watch, Trump fragrance, and a commemorative ticket and poster, according to the site. The top four VIP holders will also get 18-karat gold Trump watches, provided they hold at least 1 million TRUMP, a stake worth about $895,000 at Sunday's price.

Boxer Floyd Mayweather, "Shark Tank" investor Kevin O'Leary and NBA champion Tristan Thompson are scheduled to appear, along with mentalist Lior Suchard, according to the site. The site says there will be no private meetings with the president and that no gifts will be accepted.

TRUMP traded at $0.8950 on Sunday, down 98.8% from its all-time high of $73.43 in January 2025, just after the token launched, according to The Block's Official Trump price page. In the two weeks after this dinner was announced, TRUMP's trading volume fell about 35% and its price dropped 9%, according to Kaiko data cited by Bloomberg. Volume rose to between four and eight times its prior levels after each of the first two events were announced, Kaiko found.

Singapore-linked trading platform FastX, which reached the top of the holder leaderboard for the event, said on X on Thursday that it will attend the dinner "as an independent holder" and bring 10 questions chosen by a vote of its community. FastX has said it is not affiliated with Trump or the TRUMP project.

Morten Christensen, founder of crypto giveaways site Airdrop Alert, also plans to attend, the company said on X. Christensen, who attended both earlier events and qualified for the VIP reception, told The Wall Street Journal he bought 75,000 TRUMP at an average price of $1.70 while shorting a roughly equal amount through perpetual futures on Hyperliquid.

By holding the token and betting on its decline in price, Christensen came away with a profit of about $6, he said, due to funding fees. The same approach cost him about $1,200 for the first event and $500 for the second, he told Bloomberg. He used the same approach to win a seat for his sister.

Tron founder Justin Sun, who was the largest TRUMP holder at the May 2025 dinner, is not attending, the Journal reported, citing a person familiar with the matter. Sun sued World Liberty Financial, the Trump family's crypto venture, which operates independently of the memecoin, in April, accusing it of freezing his WLFI tokens. World Liberty has called his claims "entirely meritless."

Trump hosted the top 220 holders at the same golf club in May 2025, and the promoter held a conference and luncheon for 297 holders at Mar-a-Lago in April. Trump has not posted about the dinner or the memecoin on social media this year, per the Post. The scoring window for the promoter's next contest, a three-day Super Bowl LXI package in Los Angeles for the top 18 holders, opens on Sunday, according to the site.

Eight senators led by Sen. Adam Schiff, D-Calif., asked the White House in a letter last month to publish a list of attendees, saying the event "appears to be a pay-to-play scheme." Democrats, who won control of both chambers of Congress in this month's midterms, have pledged to investigate Trump's crypto ventures.

"The President's attendance at a privately organized event does not make it a White House event or government function," White House spokesperson Davis Ingle told Bloomberg. The White House directed questions about potential conflicts of interest to Fight Fight Fight, which did not respond to Bloomberg's request for comment.

Career staff at the Commodity Futures Trading Commission opened an inquiry into possible fraud involving TRUMP soon after its launch in early 2025, but backed off after concluding the commission would not approve subpoenas, The New York Times reported Friday, citing three former agency officials with direct knowledge of the inquiry who spoke on condition of anonymity.

The officials were concerned about possible insider trading after some early buyers posted large profits, and believed then-acting Chair Caroline Pham would not sign off, according to the Times. The inquiry did not get far enough to determine whether any wrongdoing occurred, and a CFTC spokeswoman declined to comment.

Trump collected at least $636 million in transaction and licensing fees from the memecoin last year, according to his financial disclosures, while nearly a million buyers lost a combined $3.81 billion through the end of June, according to Nansen data cited by The New York Times.

Binance obtains court orders freezing assets stolen in October’s hack. Hong Kong warns citizens about Hyperliquid, and Thai police arrest four foreigners linked to a crypto scam.

Binance has secured a $6.5 billion worldwide freezing injunction from the Abu Dhabi Global Market Courts as it seeks to recover assets stolen in its record Oct. 29 hack.

The court orders target unidentified perpetrators of the attack and are intended to help issuers, exchanges and other partners freeze and return stolen assets,

The orders can be served via blockchain, allowing legal notices to be delivered to wallet addresses rather than through traditional channels. Market participants will also be notified of updates to a “living list” of 26,000 wallet addresses identified as holding stolen assets.

Despite losing billions in the hack, Binance’s APAC head SB Seker told Reuters this week its finances are sound and it plans to expand throughout South East Asia.

“We have ongoing conversations with at least five or six markets ... three of which are in quite mature stages,” he said. Binance currently has operating licenses in 15 markets.

Hong Kong’s Securities and Futures Commission (SFC) added decentralized perps exchange Hyperliquid to its Alert List. The SFC said Hyperliquid lacks a license to operate in the city and is suspected of actively marketing its services to the region’s public.

The SFC is now officially suspicious of 69 platforms.

The Hong Kong Monetary Authority and Swift will explore linking the HKMA’s market infrastructure, Project Ensemble, with Swift’s ledger, for cross-border payments using tokenized deposits in multiple currencies.

The Monetary Authority of Singapore (MAS) issued 100 million Singapore dollars ($77.5 million) in tokenized bills on Nov. 6, with banks using wholesale central bank digital currency (CBDC) to pay for them. Five banks submitted bids totaling more than twice the amount on offer, MAS said Thursday. The four-week bills mature on Dec. 4.

Singapore’s DBS has partnered with Circle to broaden customer access to USDC across Asia.

CryptoJobsList has acquired its rival crypto jobs service Crypto.jobs.

Citadel Securities is expanding its Singapore and Sydney offices by 40% and 65% respectively.

Agribusiness solutions company Bunge and feed ingredient trading firm Bangkok Produce Merchandising Company have expanded their blockchain based soy traceability initiative to Vietnam. The collaboration has already established traceable soybean and soybean meal supply chains from Brazil to Thailand.

Thai police have arrested four foreign nationals in Pattaya over an alleged scam that defrauded victims of more than 400 million baht ($11.8M) through fake crypto investments.The operation followed the rescue of a 19-year-old Chinese student held for ransom three days earlier and uncovered a crypto romance scam allegedly run by three Chinese nationals and one Taiwanese fugitive.

#4939. A $2 million exploit leads to a $3.7 million free-for-all as traders empty Fusionist’s ACE bridge

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

A hacker minted quintillions of fake ACE tokens on BNB Chain and sold them for $2 million, crashing the price. Traders then bought the cheap tokens and swapped them for real ACE worth $3.7 million.

An attacker made off with about $2 million on Sunday by minting a massive quantity of unbacked ACE Fusionist (ACE) tokens, a creation of blockchain game project Fusionist, on BNB Chain and selling them for USDT while crashing ACE's price on the network, according to The Block's analysis of onchain data.

Traders then took the same cheap tokens, which by then numbered in the billions, and sent them through Fusionist's bridge, emptying it of its 53.7 million real ACE, worth about $3.7 million at current prices, onchain data shows. ACE's circulating supply before the exploit was only around 116 million tokens.

Fusionist is a blockchain gaming studio backed by Binance Labs (now YZi Labs) that makes sci-fi strategy games and runs its own blockchain, Endurance. ACE is the project's native token, which is used to pay transaction fees on Endurance. It trades on exchanges including Binance and OKX. A copy of the ACE token also exists as a separate token on BNB Chain. Fusionist runs a bridge that converts ACE between the two blockchains.

Onchain analytics account Onchain Lens said on X that the attacker "exploited Fusionist's privileged bridge-minter access" to mint unbacked ACE and swap it for USDT, then routed $1.87 million through crypto mixer Tornado Cash. The Block traced about 1,150 ETH, worth roughly $2 million, into Tornado Cash from wallets that either received the minted ACE directly from the bridge or collected proceeds from its sale. The attacker moved the funds from BNB Chain to Ethereum through cross-chain swap services including SwftSwap and Relay.

The first mint, of 3.5 million ACE, came at 12:55 p.m. ET, according to BscScan data. ACE has a maximum supply of 147 million tokens. The mints grew to billions, then trillions, then quadrillions of ACE over the next two and a half hours, ending with a 10 quintillion ACE mint at 3:28 p.m.

The mints were sent from a wallet that Fusionist's deployer funded nearly three years ago, BscScan data shows. Blockchain security firm Blockaid, which first flagged the exploit, listed that wallet among the exploiter addresses.

The attacker sold each batch into decentralized exchange pools on BNB Chain as soon as it was minted. By 1:41 p.m. ET, one trader was able to buy ACE there for about $0.0001, compared with the roughly $0.04 price of ACE on exchanges at the time.

Fusionist's bridge holds ACE tokens on its Endurance network to back the tokens on BNB Chain. When a user sends BNB Chain ACE back to the bridge, it burns those tokens and releases the same amount of ACE on Endurance. The bridge kept working after the attack, so any trader could buy the crashed tokens on BNB Chain and swap them for real ACE tokens, then sell them on exchanges.

From 1:13 p.m. to 3:05 p.m. ET, the Endurance contract paid out about 53.7 million ACE to 24 addresses, according to Endurance block explorer data. One address alone received 26.1 million, nearly a quarter of ACE's former circulating supply.

The bridge held about 53.7 million ACE before the hack; by Sunday afternoon it held only 0.08 ACE. Another 27 attempted transfers, totaling roughly 1.36 billion ACE, failed because the bridge was already nearly empty by that point.

Pseudonymous X user ZAMA_APPLE was the first to post about a user exploiting the arbitrage, sharing a screenshot that showed a trader turning $60 into $17,000 by buying ACE on BNB Chain and selling it on OKX. The Block traced the trade to a single wallet.

The wallet started with about $50 in USDT, withdrawn from Binance around 1:34 p.m. ET. It spent $10 testing the bridge, then used its remaining $40 to buy about 404,000 ACE at 1:41 p.m. Four minutes later, the bridge paid out the same amount of real ACE on Endurance, and the trader sent it straight to the exchange. By 1:53 p.m., it had sold the tokens for about $17,670, a 350x return.

Fusionist told its followers at 2:30 p.m. ET to pause ACE transfers, trades and bridging on BSC while it investigates "an apparent ongoing exploit." Fusionist has not posted an update since, as of publication time.

Crypto exchange Gate said its security team confirmed the bridge was exploited and that it would roll back all ACE/USDT trades executed between 12:55 p.m. and 2:15 p.m. ET. Gate also switched its ACE perpetual futures market to reduce-only and said it would settle and delist the market late Monday.

Some Gate users who sold ACE during that window were left with negative balances after Gate rolled back their trades, returning their ACE and reclaiming the USDT from the sales, Gate's support account said on X.

ACE is down about 20% over the past 24 hours, trading at roughly $0.069, according to The Block's price page. The Block has reached out to Fusionist and OKX for comment.

#4938. Morning Minute: Anthropic IPO Pops, Sinks Markets

Morning Minute: Anthropic IPO Pops, Sinks Markets

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

The largest base IPO deal ever priced at $83 a share Wednesday night, opened Thursday morning at $111.50, and briefly pushed Anthropic’s fully diluted valuation past $2 trillion. The stock finished at $97.17, up 17% from the offer and down 13% from where it opened (it has since fallen about 15% to $88).

A 17% first-day gain on an $81.34 billion raise is a successful listing by any normal standard.

The rest of the market did not fare as well.

The Nasdaq fell 2.06%. The S&P 500 dropped 1.9%. The Dow lost 2.17% and closed below 50,000 for the first time since June. Tech had rallied before the bell on the listing and reversed by the afternoon.

MD fell 7.73%. Nebius dropped 7.8%. CoreWeave lost 5%. Nvidia slid 3.4% despite beating earnings last week. Oracle, Micron, Broadcom and Microsoft all finished lower. The biggest AI listing in history happened, and AI infrastructure was the worst place to be holding.

Joe Maher at Capital Economics put it plainly: “Anthropic’s record-breaking IPO has failed to rekindle enthusiasm for tech stocks.”

Crypto went with it. Bitcoin fell to $61,500, its lowest since early July. That’s a seventh straight weekly loss, which would be the longest run since the nine-week stretch in 2022. Eth hit the $1,750 level, down 28% in thirty days. Gold fell to a fourteen-month low. Silver dropped 3%.

When Bitcoin, equities and gold all sell off on the same session, the common factor is usually the discount rate.

That’s the Fed. Chris Waller spent this week making the case for fifty basis points in December, and the minutes showed he isn’t alone. FxPro’s Alex Kuptsikevich described the market as spooked by “the spectre of a double rate hike” and pointed out Bitcoin is now more than 7% below its 200-week moving average, the deepest it has traded below that line in three years.

So where does this leave us heading into next week?

Start with what an $81 billion IPO actually does to a market. That money came from somewhere. Add the separate $26 billion private placement from Nvidia, Google, AMD and four other suppliers, and roughly $107 billion of capital got committed to a single name in one day. Kuptsikevich raised the possibility that the listing absorbed the riskiest money in the system, and that if so, some of it rotates back out once the allocation settles.

That’s the bull case for crypto over the next few weeks. It requires the Fed to cooperate.

For the bear case, one can simply look at SpaceX.

SpaceX priced at $135 in June, opened at $150, and closed its first day up 19%. Then it kept running, hitting $225.64 four days later, up 67% from the offer. The unwind started the following session. By June 22 it had fallen 16% in a single day and sat 31% off the high. By mid-July it traded below its IPO price. By late July it was down roughly 50% from the peak.

That would project out to a market low around Christmas time, which is actually how the 2018 midterm cycle played out. Will history repeat itself? We will find out soon enough. In the interim, expect volatility.

#4935. Ledger CTO Charles Guillemet to leave after nine years at company

Guillemet helped establish Ledger’s Donjon security lab and says his departure has been planned for months.

Ledger chief technology officer Charles Guillemet announced he will be leaving the company at the end of 2026, after nine years at the company.

In a post on LinkedIn on Thursday, Guillemet said he joined as chief security officer in December 2017, around three years after Ledger was founded. He launched Ledger Donjon, the company’s attack lab, a year later.

“We’ve been preparing this transition for many months, so Ledger’s teams, partners and roadmaps are ready. I’m bullish on Ledger’s future,” said Guillemet.

Guillemet has been a central figure in Ledger’s security operations, first as chief security officer and later as chief technology officer. He helped establish the Ledger Donjon attack lab, which tests hardware and software vulnerabilities. According to Guillemet, Ledger devices now secure roughly 20% of the world’s crypto assets.

“The threat landscape changed too. AI is making attacks cheaper, faster and easier to scale,” he added.

“The question we spent a decade answering for crypto (how do you protect what matters when any device can be compromised?) is now a question for every company, every AI agent and every government.”

Guillemet did not disclose details of his next venture but said he was “building something new” and would “dedicate my time to solving this security mess.”

Guillemet’s announcement comes weeks after Ledger disclosed that attackers had modified genuine hardware wallets with hidden components designed to capture users’ recovery phrases, and slipped the devices into authorized resellers.

The tampering cases were initially reported in Asia, with subsequent incidents identified in Europe and the United Arab Emirates.

#4934. What to Wear to Thanksgiving: 6 Outfit Ideas for Every Type of Gathering

From dressier holiday looks to comfy and stretchy.

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

With Thanksgiving just around the corner, I’ve already started thinking about two very important things: what’s going on the dinner table and what I’ll wear. I’m especially looking forward to my mom’s baked mashed potatoes (a family favorite) and the homemade mac and cheese I’ll be bringing along. But, especially as a style writer, half the fun of the holidays is having a good excuse to put together a good outfit.

My family’s Thanksgiving gatherings tend to be pretty casual, so I’m not one to show up in anything overly dressy or uncomfortable. I’m usually somewhere in the middle, opting for an outfit that looks like I made an effort but still lets me enjoy a second helping of stuffing without worrying about a too-tight waistband. Think a great sweater with loose-fitting jeans, a knit dress with boots, or even a smartly-styled leggings outfit. Because as much as I love dressing up for the holidays, I also want to enjoy post-dinner card games or sit comfortably on the couch afterward.

If you’re approaching Thanksgiving dressing the same way, I’ve rounded up six outfit ideas to help get you inspired. I’ve included a few options perfect for a Christmas card photoshoot, along with more laid-back looks that are just as fitting for a casual family gathering. No matter what your holiday plans look like, these outfits strike that happy medium between looking good and feeling comfortable. Now, if you’ll excuse me, I have some mac and cheese to make.

In my opinion, there’s no outfit more perfect for the holidays than a sweater dress and boots. It’s cute enough for family photos but loose and soft enough to keep you comfy all day (especially if you kick the boots off at the door). Grab a long-sleeve knit dress that hits somewhere between mid-thigh and the knee and pair it with some fleece-lined tights and your fave knee-high boot. A midi or maxi dress with an ankle bootie works too.

Start by pulling out a denim jacket and denim skirt. From there, add a plain white T-shirt. I love brown for the fall, so I wore my version of this outfit with brown boots and a matching brown belt and bag. I love this combo because it looks super stylish, but I don’t feel overdressed or uncomfortable when I wear it. Don’t have a denim skirt or jacket? No problem. You can recreate this look with a leather jacket, jeans, or any neutral mini skirt.

Start with a pair of satin or silk pants and pair them with a sweater and ballet flats in the same shade. This outfit has been one of my go-tos lately because it’s so comfortable, but the monochrome palette and shiny finish of the pants make it look like I tried way harder than I actually did. You can even make it feel a little dressier by swapping the flats for a pair of pointed-toe ankle boots.

It doesn’t get comfier than leggings and slippers. For ultra-casual gatherings when you just want to focus on family and food without looking frumpy, just style yours a little smarter. Go for a pair of leggings with minimal seaming (especially a pair without a front seam), which makes them look sleeker. Then grab a dressy-looking oversized sweater—something with a boxy cut, a turtleneck, or a nice knit. I find that my tweed stitch J. Crew Rollneck Sweater is perfect for this look. Finish it off with a neutral slipper and a pair of scrunchy socks.

Crz Yoga SculptAir No Front Seam Leggings

Opt for a pair of baggier jeans for this more casual outfit, which will be way comfier than a restrictive skinny or straight-leg jean. A looser pant also pairs better with a cozy boot, like this platform ankle boot. Finally, throw on a long-sleeve top or sweater with a cute vest for an outfit that feels very fall.

Banana Republic The Icon Classic Wide-Leg Jean

Okay, you caught me—I actually wore this outfit for Christmas last year instead of Thanksgiving, but it definitely works for either holiday! For this combo, start with your favorite skirt and a turtleneck top. Then top it off with a relaxed-fitting blazer (I love this houndstooth blazer, but any pattern or solid color will do). On the bottom, throw on a pair of tights and a knee-high boot.

#4932. Spot bitcoin ETFs shed $1.8 billion in past week as outflows become ‘structural’: analysts

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Bitcoin fell to a cycle low near $55,100 over the weekend amid continued risk-off sentiment, macro headwinds, and an AI equities-led stock market downturn.

Spot bitcoin exchange-traded funds in the U.S. reported around $1.79 billion in net outflows last week, the largest weekly withdrawal since the week ended June 26, 2026.

This marked the fourth consecutive week of outflows for the funds, according to data from SoSoValue. BlackRock's iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT) led the outflows with $1.37 billion alone during the week.

So far this month, around $4.14 billion has left bitcoin ETFs, trailing June's record monthly net outflows. Total net assets in the bitcoin ETFs sat at $71.35 billion as of Friday, down from around $110 billion in mid-September.

Spot ether Ethereum (ETH) ETFs also reported $314 million in weekly outflows, making it the eighth consecutive week of negative flows.

Laevitas Head of Markets Rick Maeda told The Block that the current net outflow represents "mostly risk reduction" among investors. "Profit-taking usually follows a rally, but these outflows have come into a falling market, with BTC sliding from around $80,000 at the start of the month to the low $60,000s," said Maeda.

Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) fell to a cycle low near $55,700 over the weekend amid continued risk-off sentiment, macro headwinds, and an AI equities-led stock market downturn. The Nasdaq Composite fell around 10% last week, and the S&P 500 dropped 8.6% as the scandal involving former Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang fueled wider concerns about the sustainability of the AI sector. The Block's Bitcoin Fear and Greed Index currently shows 11 — extreme fear.

"Being on the far end of the risk asset spectrum, whilst also facing its own headwinds such as quantum computing and hacks, crypto has been an easy asset for investors to get rid of from their portfolios," Maeda added.

Dan Khus, chief analyst at LVRG Research, said the current ETF outflow streak appears to be more structural than previous outflows.

"Unlike earlier outflow periods that were often shorter and more reactive to single events, the current four-week streak appears more structural, coinciding with sustained price pressure, elevated liquidations, and a shift in institutional positioning toward capital preservation," Khus commented.

Risk-averse investors are moving further away from crypto products as they expect the Federal Reserve to deliver another interest rate hike in December, analysts said. The U.S. Personal Consumption Expenditures (PCE) Price Index, scheduled to be announced this Wednesday, may hint at how sticky inflation remains, and whether the Federal Reserve will proceed with another rate hike in December, BTSE COO Jeff Mei said.

"Bitcoin and ether ETFs may face further outflows as rate-hike concerns and weak risk appetite weigh on institutional demand," Zeus Research Analyst Dominick John said. "Bitcoin is likely to remain more resilient, while Ether faces greater pressure. A sustained recovery will depend on easing macro fears and renewed inflows."

#4931. Institutions pile back into US Bitcoin ETFs in Q3 after selling streak

Asset managers added the equivalent of 49,229 Bitcoin to their US Bitcoin ETF holdings in the third quarter, reversing three quarters of net selling.

Institutional investment managers’ holdings of US Bitcoin exchange-traded funds climbed to a record high in the third quarter, as they reversed three straight quarters of net selling, according to regulatory filings.

The 13F filings, analyzed by Galaxy Research, show that the reporting companies added an equivalent of 49,229 Bitcoin (BTC) in the third quarter, outpacing the 47,054 Bitcoin they shed over the last three quarters combined. This lifted the reported total holdings to 356,491 BTC, the highest on record.

“The quarter’s accumulation was the work of a few dozen large institutions,” wrote Galaxy’s head of firmwide research Alex Thorn.

Quarterly 13F filings provide a point-in-time snapshot of US-listed securities held by reporting institutional investment managers. Thorn said half of the net addition of spot Bitcoin ETF shares came from advisors and wealth platforms.

Wells Fargo posted the largest increase, adding the equivalent of 14,636 Bitcoin, making it the largest institutional holder of spot Bitcoin ETPs. Other strong gainers included Brevan Howard, Bank of America, Millennium Management and Jane Street.

Largest holders of spot Bitcoin ETP positions in Q3. Source: Galaxy Research

“Early readings suggest longer-term holders make up a growing share of the spot Bitcoin ETF investor base,” Bitwise analyst Camran Khosravi previously told Cointelegraph, when the first batch of 13F filings had begun to come in.

The number of managers reporting a position in a spot Bitcoin ETF also rose for the first time in three quarters, increasing by 191 to 2,166.

This all came as Bitcoin gained about 43% in the third quarter, ending September at $83,621.

Related: Bitcoin ETF assets fall to three-month low as 2026 net outflows top $2.5B

Meanwhile, Galaxy’s data shows BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust accounted for the largest share of holdings reported by 13F filers, with the equivalent of 232,453 BTC held by the filers. This was followed by the Fidelity Wise Origin Bitcoin Fund.

Together, the two funds were responsible for 77% of all Bitcoin held by the reporting entities.

Bitcoin equivalent held by 13F filers. Source: Galaxy Research

The record institutional holdings in Q3 contrast with more recent weakness in US spot Bitcoin ETFs, with net monthly outflows reaching $515 million in October and $2.9 billion of outflows month to date, according to TFTC’s Bitcoin ETF tracker.

Earlier in the month, Bitwise head of research André Dragosch told Cointelegraph he viewed the selling as “related to a short-term risk-off environment rather than a structural change in institutional sentiment.”

#4930. Senate Passes Bill Banning Prediction Market Bets on War, Terrorism and Death

Senate Passes Bill Banning Prediction Market Bets on War, Terrorism and Death

The Senate has passed a bill that would ban prediction markets from offering bets on war, terrorism, assassinations and deaths, sending the measure to the House of Representatives.

The Discouraging Exploitative Assassination, Tragedy, and Harm Betting in Event Trading Systems Act, or DEATH BETS Act, passed the Senate by voice vote on Wednesday, November 19, with an amendment, according to Congress.gov. The bill was introduced by Sen. Adam Schiff (D-CA) in March, and is cosponsored by Sen. John Curtis (R-UT).

"With this bill, Congress is making it clear that these bets should be illegal," Schiff tweeted after the vote.

BREAKING: The Senate just passed my bill with @SenJohnCurtis to prohibit prediction markets from listing contracts involving terrorism, assassination, war, military operations, or an individual’s death.

With this bill, Congress is making it clear that these bets should be… pic.twitter.com/Mzu5PWmGsL

— Senator Adam Schiff (@SenAdamSchiff) November 20, 2026

The bill would amend the Commodity Exchange Act to bar exchanges and clearinghouses registered with the Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC) from listing or clearing event contracts that expressly reference terrorism, assassination, war or U.S. military operations.

It would also ban contracts that, at the time of listing, are "reasonably expected to resolve principally based on a death of an individual." The CFTC would be able to extend the ban to "any similar activity" by rule.

The bill would not affect the status of other types of contracts covered by existing CFTC rules on event contracts, such as those involving gaming, according to its text.

Prediction markets such as Kalshi and Polymarket let users trade on the outcome of real-world events, from elections and sports to geopolitics. Markets on wars and the fates of world leaders have drawn criticism from lawmakers, who have argued they let people profit from tragedy, and could reward insiders with knowledge of military plans. Polymarket users have been accused of insider trading after making $2.4 million on Iran-related predictions. Polymarket pulled a market on whether a nuclear weapon would be detonated after a public backlash.

Senate Democrats have raised concerns that people close to President Donald Trump could profit from war-related markets, and one senator has alleged White House corruption over Iran war predictions. Kalshi was also sued after refusing to pay out on a market tied to the death of Iran's then-Supreme Leader, Ali Khamenei.

The CFTC has also moved to curb such markets. In June, it proposed rules that would ban markets on outcomes that could come about through war or assassination, such as vaguely worded bets on when U.S. adversaries would be "out of office," which Kalshi and Polymarket had used to sidestep existing bans on war-related contracts.

War-related markets have also appeared elsewhere, including on Myriad, the prediction market operated by Decrypt's parent company DASTAN, where, where users traded on whether U.S. troops would enter Iran.

The bill's passage comes as prediction markets face growing legal pressure from states, which argue that some event contracts amount to unlicensed gambling. New York, for example, has sued Coinbase and Gemini over their prediction markets. The CFTC, which first sued three states in April to defend its authority over the markets, has now taken 12 states to court, most recently Louisiana.

#4928. The 7 Best Facial Hair Removal Tools, Tested by Editors

Walk don’t run to Amazon because our best overall recommendation is 26% off RN.

There are tons of different facial hair removal methods for women, including laser hair removal, waxing, and epilators. I have tried them all, including electric razors and dermaplaning tools. As WH’s beauty editor, I've sugared my face and body and interviewed dozens of derms about preventing dreaded razor bumps and unsightly ingrown hairs. Some facial hair removal methods take only a few minutes, while others can be pretty painful but require minimal upkeep. Finding the right method or combination of methods that work for your needs might take a few tries, but I've got you covered with my beauty editor-vetted recommendations. These editor-tested and dermatologist-recommended suggestions start at just $10.

Yes, this is the priciest recommendation, but it's totally worth the splurge. Braun works in just one treatment session and boasts smart features that justify the added cost and our top spot. The idea of gliding a sharp razor across my face was initially intimidating, but I haven't scratched myself since using the Dermaflash. My results rival pro treatments without the hassle of going to a spa and without the excessive upkeep cost. This tool easily glides across my face, which would normally be a challenge with cheaper, manual dermaplaning tools. Since using this device, I noticed my face cream sinks in quickly, and my foundation looks even more natural.

I try to use this tool every two weeks to maintain my smooth results, but TBH, I use it monthly, which is better for my wallet. The brand advises swapping out the blade after every use for the smoothest results, so anything to stretch my beauty budget is always a win. FWIW: Even a monthly treatment prevents my makeup from pilling, so this tool will remain a consistent part of my routine.

#4926. Anthropic’s $81B IPO draws half the net retail buying of SpaceX’s

Retail investors traded $772 million in Anthropic shares on Thursday, the most Vanda Research has recorded for a market debut since 2012.

Retail investors traded $772 million in Anthropic shares on its Nasdaq debut Thursday, but their net purchases totaled just $60 million, about half the $118 million they put into SpaceX on its first day of trading in June.

Anthropic’s first-day retail trading volume topped SpaceX’s $669 million and was the highest for any market debut in Vanda Research’s data, which go back to 2012, Yahoo Finance reported.

Volume counts both purchases and sales, while net buying measures how much purchases exceeded sales.

The Claude AI developer and its existing shareholders offered 980 million shares at $83 each in the $81.34 billion initial public offering (IPO). Anthropic shares opened on Nasdaq at $111.50 on Thursday, 34.3% above the IPO price, and closed at $97.17 for a first-day gain of 17.1%.

SpaceX shares gained 19.2% in their June 12 debut, closing at $160.95 against an IPO price of $135, according to Nasdaq. Although Anthropic’s base offering exceeded SpaceX’s initial $75 billion sale, SpaceX ultimately raised $85.7 billion after underwriters exercised their option to purchase additional shares.

Before the IPO, Vanda forecast about $250 million in net retail buying, more than four times Thursday’s total. Its forecast drew on retail trading in past market debuts and the volume of Anthropic perpetual futures traded on decentralized exchange Hyperliquid.

Shortly before Nasdaq trading began, those futures traded at $110.48, just 0.9% below the stock’s opening price.

#4921. Bitcoin Breaks Below $60K as Crypto Fear Index Hits Worst Level Since February

Bitcoin Breaks Below $60K as Crypto Fear Index Hits Worst Level Since February

Bitcoin broke below $60,000 on Friday, plunging through a major psychological price level as the selloff gathered pace after Anthropic’s stock market debut.

The daily chart shows BTC at $59,703.72 , down 3.55% for the session after touching a low of $59,300.

The Fear and Greed Index chart dipped to 9, a reading classified as “extreme fear” and its lowest since February. For reference, the index was at 18 a week ago and a neutral 51 a month earlier.

You can now act on the news you read: Go long or short Bitcoin with perpetual futures, all from your Decrypt Money Account.

The indicator tracks crypto market sentiment on a scale from zero, or maximum fear, to 100, or extreme greed, using market data rather than individual investor surveys. Its 2026 low was 5 on February 5, compared with a yearly high of 82 on August 26.

The selling went well beyond Bitcoin. Ethereum fell 4.24%, Solana lost 6.36%, BNB slid 4.38% and XRP declined 5.53% over the same period.

Forced selling added to the pressure. As Decrypt reported earlier Friday, roughly $727 million in leveraged crypto positions had been liquidated over 24 hours, about 86% of them bullish bets, while U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs lost $582.2 million on Thursday.

Spot fund withdrawals did not begin with Anthropic’s IPO. By Thursday, roughly $2.9 billion in U.S. Bitcoin ETF outflows for November, alongside higher Treasury yields and expectations of tighter Federal Reserve policy.

Anthropic made its Nasdaq debut Thursday at $111.50, up 34% from the $83 IPO price, before closing at $97.17. Friday brought another decline for the Claude developer, with the stock almost losing all the prime it gained after its IPO.

The listing raised $81.34 billion through the public offering, but enthusiasm for Anthropic shares did not carry over to the companies building the AI boom.

Friday’s market heatmap shows tech stock suffering in the middle of mostly green pastures. Nvidia down 3.78%, Broadcom is down 5.65%, AMD declining 5.06% and memory-chip maker Micron dropping 6.51%. Meta fell 2.21%, adding to pressure on companies spending heavily on AI infrastructure.

Other major stocks were doing better. Alphabet rose 2.81%, Microsoft gained 0.50% and Apple edged up 0.25%, while healthcare stocks Eli Lilly and Johnson & Johnson rose 1.21% and 1.78%, respectively. Walmart added 2.91% and Visa climbed 1.34%.

But it was not a uniform crash across the S&P 500. The tech-heavy Nasdaq was falling more sharply than the broader index, while pockets of healthcare, consumer, and financial shares remained green.

Bitcoin’s daily chart turned more decisively bearish after the break below $60,000. Its 50-day exponential moving average, or EMA, was below the 200-day EMA, a death-cross configuration, and spot prices were trading beneath both lines. That confirms sustained downside momentum rather than a brief intraday dip.

The relative strength index, or RSI, had fallen into the teens, far below the 30 threshold usually associated with oversold conditions. The average directional index, or ADX, climbed to 43.7, signaling a strong trend, with the chart’s directional readings favoring sellers. Oversold does not mean a bounce is guaranteed.

The longer-range chart puts the $60,000 breakdown in context. Bitcoin traded around this area several times in 2024 and returned to it during the mid-2026 retreat, making it a visible historical price zone as well as a round-number threshold.

With $60,000 giving way, the roughly $58,000–$59,000 support area near the mid-2026 lows comes into focus. If that zone fails, the next notable historical demand area is around $55,000–$56,000, followed by the $50,000 region marked by the deeper 2024 selloff.

For bulls, $60,000 is now the first level to hold. The next hurdle is $62,000–$62,500, followed by $65,000 and heavier resistance near $68,000–$70,000. A brief bounce back above $60,000 would not, by itself, reverse the bearish trend.

As of right now, Bitcoin had touched $59,300.13, confirming that sellers had pushed below the $60,000 support zone. The next test is whether that level becomes resistance on a rebound.

No pressure, though—if you try swallowing and don’t like it, you never have to try it again. You might say, “Hey, it’s totally fine if you cum near my mouth, but I don’t think I can swallow. That cool?” (And if it’s not, you should probably reconsider your offer of fellatio. No one thinks about wearing a condom for a blow job, but practicing safe oral sex is just as important as practicing safe penetrative sex. “Using condoms during fellatio protects against sexually transmitted infections (STIs), offering both partners safety for a more relaxed and enjoyable experience,” Lawless says. “They can also aid in simplifying the cleanup process.” Luckily, there are flavored condoms that can make the experience more pleasant.

“They taste really good, and the lubricant used is PH-balanced, FDA-cleared, and has no spermicide,” sex therapist Rachel Wright, LMFT, previously told Women’s Health. “They’re also made with sustainably-sourced, natural rubber latex, which many flavored condoms are not.”

The stakes can feel high when you're going down, but try to release control over whether the receiver ejaculates or how long the BJ takes. “Focusing on the journey of pleasure—both in receiving and giving—is a key piece of satisfying your partner,” Skyler says.

Not to mention, “it reduces performance anxiety for both partners,” adds Lawless, “leading to a more satisfying and intimate encounter.”

One thing that’s easy to forget when giving a blow job is the cleanup phase! Not super fun, necessarily, but still important—especially because, given your outstanding work, there might be something to wipe up.

If you want to elevate the experience, consider leaving for a moment and coming back with two washcloths, she adds: “One as a hot towel to erotically wipe your partner down, and another to fluff them dry. It can be a fun way to offer a full-service treatment!”

While the receiver might be basking in a post-blowjob glow, it's important for both partners to make time for aftercare. This is crucial for all intimate encounters, but especially ones that are as vulnerable as oral sex.

“Aftercare is a great way to follow fellatio,” Lawless says. After cleaning up, this might look like fostering emotional intimacy through cuddling, or it might take the more practical form of using mouthwash or brushing your teeth. There's no wrong way to do aftercare so long as “it helps partners nurture each other, take care of each other, and transition from being sexual humans to going back into their everyday lives,” psychologist and sex therapist Kate Balestrieri, PsyD, previously told Women's Health.

Communication can be a crucial part of aftercare—with benefits that last far longer than the average BJ. “Talk about what was great and what you may want to avoid next time to up your blow job game,” says Lawless. “Great lovers always share preferences and feedback to help make sex pleasurable and fun.”

#4919. SEC charges 8 firms over fake crypto platforms tied to $37M fraud

The SEC says the operators showed investors fake certificates and pointed them to filings on the agency’s website as proof of regulatory approval.

The US Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) charged eight Colorado-based entities Thursday, alleging they used fake crypto trading platforms to take $37.4 million from more than 1,500 US investors.

Four of the defendants posed as investment advisers, and each steered investors to a fake trading platform run by its partner, according to the SEC’s complaint. The four pairs operated in succession from August 2024 to May 2026.

The defendants allegedly lured investors by masquerading as investment advisers or representatives of crypto asset trading platforms, which the SEC described as fronts designed to steal investor money, the agency announced in a Thursday press release.

The SEC said the operators were likely overseas and used social media ads to lure investors into private chat groups. They first offered stock-trading advice before persuading victims to transfer money to fake crypto platforms, where victims believed they were buying genuine crypto assets.

The SEC previously warned investors that scammers used investment-related group chats to promote fraud schemes, including by impersonating financial professionals, according to a December 2025 alert.

The scheme allegedly operated through four successive adviser-platform pairs from August 2024 to May 2026, with operators switching pairs every few months.

The eight entities charged were Advisor WeChat Capital, Tech Brokerage Asset Alliance Ltd., HB Wealth Advisors, IdeaCrypto Exchange LLC, Convergent Wealth Advisors Inc., RealSimple Crypto Exchange Inc., Associated Wealth Advisors Ltd., and Essential Crypto Exchange.

“The SEC continues to seek out and expose fraudulent investment scams that prey on U.S. investors,” said Laura D’Allaird, chief of the SEC Enforcement Division’s Cyber and Emerging Technologies Unit, encouraging the public to report such schemes through its online portal.

During fiscal year 2026, the SEC filed 348 enforcement actions, down 23.7% from 456 a year earlier. The SEC filed 13 crypto-related actions in calendar year 2025, down from 33 in 2024, according to Cornerstone Research.

Cotton may be the most dependable fabric for regular every-day t-shirts, but cotton holds onto moisture, which makes it a sweat trap, weighing you down. Instead, opt for synthetic, moisture-wicking fabrics like polyester and nylon that are lighter. They can also be woven in special ways that cotton can't be to enhance breathability. Synthetic fibers don't bleed and are hard to discolor, so they won't get sweat stains. Some menswear brands are even starting to use finely knit wool for its natural breathability, moisture wicking, and odor-fighting properties. Consider what feels best to you and in what environment you might be working out in.

In most clothing, seams are placed where they are easiest to construct the shirt or look the best. However, those places—like under your armpit and along the side of your body—are also high friction areas, especially when working out. Athletic brands have designed t-shirts that minimize the amount of seams and place them in areas that wont be impacted by your movement. Look out for seams and other high-contact areas that could prose a problem, depending on where they're located.

Simply put, your workout clothes get stinky when they hold on to sweat. A whole range of innovations exist that brands claim will fight odor, but the only sure fire way to keep your workout shirts from stinking are to keep them dry and wash them after every use. That means the workout shirts that retain the least amount of sweat are the easiest to keep from stinking. So if odor is a concern for you (as it should be), look for the shirts that dry the fastest and breath the best. There's a lot of new tech out now that helps combat stink.

Forward thinking labels are adding UV protection to workout clothes. Sunscreen is great, but even the best sweat-proof kinds can wear off during long and intense outdoor exercises. Not so with UPF clothing, which block UV rays to keep you protected during your entire sweat session, no reapplication necessary. Even the Skin Cancer Foundation has touted the skin-saving benefits of UPF clothing. If you're looking for even more sun protection, look for long-sleeve t-shirts, or hooded tops with UPF features.

Synthetic fabrics work especially well in workout shirts, as they're designed to handle moisture better than traditional materials. These fabrics include: polyester, Gore-Tex, nylon, and spandex, which have tiny spaces in knit or mesh that allow moisture to move through, evaporating quickly.

According to Dr. Corey L. Hartman, MD, an assistant clinical professor of dermatology at the University of Alabama School of Medicine, moisture-wicking fabrics help moisture from the inner layer of the fabric (the part that touches your skin) escape to the outside of the garment.

"Synthetic materials are better at moisture-wicking as they not only pull sweat moisture away from the skin, but they also dry quickly and don’t hold onto moisture," explains. Dr. Marisa Garshick, board-certified dermatologist of MDCS Dermatology.

"Cotton, on the other hand, while it can absorb sweat and moisture from the skin, it is more likely to hold onto the moisture and not dry as quickly, leaving the sweat in prolonged contact with the skin," she explains. Although, some cotton fabrics may be specially treated to make them moisture wicking.

Double checking the tags on your tops, or reading about the materials it's made of will help you pick proper shirts for your workouts. And thankfully, you can find moisture-wicking materials in tanks, short sleeve tees, long sleeve tees and hoodies.

So, why buy a workout shirt anyway? We've all worked out out in a regular cotton t-shirt at one time or another, and while it certainly won't ruin your gym session, it won't make it more enjoyable either.

The result of wearing shirts designed for athletic performance is that the fabric from which they're made feels drier and cooler against the skin.

"Moisture-wicking [shirts] can be especially beneficial when the body is subject to higher temperatures, like during a workout or a hot summer day," Dr. Hartman explains. Plus, keeping things cool and dry under your shirt can actually minimize a slew of issues.

"Buildup of sweat can lead to irritation which can appear as red, flaky patches or breakouts, which appear as red papules or pustules that may involve the hair follicle," explains Dr. Garshick.

There are some body odor perks, too (or lack thereof), according to Dr. Hartman. "Body odor occurs when sweat mixes with natural bacteria found on the skin. The less sweat on the skin, the less body odor."

#4916. Wrapped Monero Token Collapses 99% After $13 Million Hack Blamed on Web Host

Wrapped Monero Token Collapses 99% After $13 Million Hack Blamed on Web Host

A wrapped version of Monero lost nearly all its value Friday after an attacker drained its deployer wallet and dumped the tokens on Hyperliquid.

XMR1, a token issued by cross-chain swap service Wagyu that represents Monero on Hyperliquid, fell more than 99% in roughly half an hour, from about $500 to around $1.

The account that deployed the token sent 5.2 million XMR1 to a newly created account early Friday, according to an analysis by onchain data firm Bitquery.

You can now act on the news you read: Go long or short Bitcoin with perpetual futures, all from your Decrypt Money Account.

That account sold about 364,000 of the tokens to 388 buyers for $13.5 million, then converted the proceeds to ETH. At Bitquery’s last check, about 7,350 ETH sat in three wallets.

Wagyu’s pseudonymous founder, PerpetualCow, blamed a breach at 1984.hosting, an Icelandic web hosting provider.

He wrote on X that the XMR1 deployer key had been stored on an old server there that he had deleted, but that the host apparently kept its logs and files. “Lesson to learn here is that these hosting providers keep your data/logs even after you delete your server with them,” he wrote.

1984 ehf., the company behind the service, said it had suffered an extensive and serious cyberattack on its IT infrastructure, Icelandic outlet DV reported Friday, citing a joint statement with CERT-IS, Iceland’s national cybersecurity incident response team.

Early findings point to a well-organized and technically sophisticated attack, and the company said it couldn’t rule out that more systems were affected than it had confirmed. It temporarily shut down all services. The statement didn’t mention Wagyu or whether customer data was taken.

Turns out that the entire hosting provider of https://t.co/skXiWey4Fb got hacked with everyone's data + internal server files today.

The XMR1 deployer key was held on an OLD server on there that I DELETED, but…

— PerpetualCow.hl (@PerpetualCow) November 20, 2026

PerpetualCow said he would personally refund everyone who lost money and that Wagyu would relaunch later. “Give me some time please,” he wrote, but didn’t give a timeline.

Bitquery said onchain data alone can’t show whether the deployer’s key was stolen, and that it can’t see how much Monero backs the remaining XMR1, since the Monero side of the bridge isn’t visible.

The attacker’s account received its first funds from the crypto mixer Tornado Cash minutes before it was set up. The same deployer account still controls nearly all of ZEC1, a Zcash token. Wagyu also disclosed a separate data leak in October, saying at the time that funds were unaffected.

Wagyu surfaced earlier this month in ZachXBT’s tracing of a suspected $106 million theft from an unnamed European entity, in which part of the funds were converted to Monero through the service.

#4913. BlackRock’s IBIT loses record $541M amid Anthropic market debut

BlackRock’s IBIT posted its largest daily outflow on record, taking three-session outflows near $900 million as BTC dipped amid a broader market selloff.

US spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds (ETFs) recorded $582 million in net outflows on Thursday, led by a record withdrawal from BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT).

IBIT posted $541 million in outflows on Thursday, its largest single-day withdrawal since launching in January 2024, according to Farside Investors data. The figure surpassed its previous daily record of $528.3 million, set on Jan. 30, 2026.

IBIT shares closed Thursday at $35.08, down 0.8% for the session. The shares have fallen 24.2% over the past month and 29.4% year to date, according to Yahoo Finance.

Thursday marked IBIT’s third consecutive trading day of outflows, totaling nearly $900 million. The latest streak followed a single day of inflows on Monday, which ended 11 straight sessions of withdrawals totaling roughly $2.35 billion.

The selling brought US spot Bitcoin ETFs’ November outflows to $3.47 billion, pushing their 2026 net withdrawals to approximately $3.11 billion, according to SoSoValue data.

Bitcoin (BTC) traded at around $61,875 at publishing time, down 3% over the past 24 hours, according to CoinGecko. The crypto asset was down 9.8% over the past seven days.

The ETF withdrawals coincided with a broader market selloff Thursday, as Anthropic’s highly anticipated stock market debut failed to sustain gains in AI-related stocks, including Nvidia, amid concerns over high valuations and heavy spending on artificial intelligence.
